Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (the fold's order, how much folds, the menu) and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit at several widths. Not checked with a real screen reader, or in Firefox or Safari.
- `POST /api/call/join`, which the page asks just before joining the call, for the plan's calls cap. A signed-in person sends `{ space }` (the Lobby when left out; the same membership rule as `POST /api/token`); a guest sends `{ guest }`, their guest link's token (in the body or as `?guest=`), and joins that link's space. It answers 200 `{ ok: true }`; 401 "sign in first"; 404 "no such space" or "that guest link is off or wrong"; 403 "you are not in that space", "that guest link is for another space", or the calls cap's sentence. Joining an aside's call is never refused here: the cap was asked when the aside was made. Documented in `architecture-overview.md` and `architecture-environments.md`. Checked by `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` against a stand-in LiveKit, and by curl.
- The word on the button for entering a space is set by the environment's template, and an owner can change it: **Enter** by default, since entering a space doesn't join the call (Thomas, 2026-09-30; addendum 4 of `plan-environment-templates.md`). It is the space list's main button and its pop-out button ("<word> in a pop-out window"), and the guest form's heading and button. Owners set it on Manage > **Template** > **Words**, in a last row, **Button for entering a space**: one field, blank for the template's word or Enter, "From the template" or "The template's: <word>" when the template gives one, saved with the words, and **Reset**. The host admin sets a template's word in the host template editor, under **Words**, and the compare panel lists **Verbs**. The Travel template sets none, so a trip reads Enter. For developers: a `verbs` set beside `words`, one key, `enter`, resolved as the owner's (`settings.verbs.enter`), then the template's, then the default. `GET /api/branding` carries `verbs`; `GET` and `PATCH /api/settings` also give the owner `ownVerbs` (`{}` for none) and `templateVerbs` (null with no template, `{}` for a template without verbs). `PATCH /api/settings` `{ verbs: { enter: "<text>" | null } }` is owner only; null goes back to the template's or the default; the text is tidied, 1 to 20 characters of letters, spaces, hyphens and apostrophes, and a refused verb refuses the whole patch with one of "Verbs must be given by name, each as text.", "There is no verb called <key>; the verbs are enter.", "The enter verb must be text, or null to use the default.", "The enter verb must be 1 to 20 characters." or "The enter verb can use only letters, spaces, hyphens and apostrophes.". A template's optional `verbs` field is live: no version bump, not in the switch's offer or the applied-once fingerprint, and in the whole-template fingerprint only when set, so no edited bundled template's notice opens for it; in a template file a verb that isn't text, null included, is refused with "verbs: The enter verb must be text.", and an export leaves unset verbs out (`format` and `formatVersion` unchanged). The host editor's `PATCH` takes `verbs`, and null removes it. Not in `host.locale()`. Pages: `verb()`, `verbs()` and `setVerbs()` in `public/words.js` (re-exported by `brand.js`), `data-verb="enter"`, `{enter}` in `fill()` and `data-fill`, kept in the browser as `app.verbs`. Space settings' launch-link hint reads the word ("Shown on the button next to <word>…"). No module version changes. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` ("Templates"), `architecture-overview.md` ("Words"), `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-templates.md`, `userguide-spaces.md`, `userguide-accounts.md` and `userguide-call.md`; the plan's addendum 4 says built. Verified live on a single install and with `BASE_DOMAIN`, in headless Chromium and by curl: the defaults, an owner's word everywhere, **Reset**, a host template's word reaching an environment at once, every refusal, the guest form, and the space list at 1280, 1024 and 390 pixels wide with long words. Checked by tools: `tools/check-templates.mjs` (34 groups), `tools/check-template-switch.mjs` (14), `tools/check-names.mjs --words` and `tools/check-slot-names.mjs`. Not checked: **Back to <space>** in a real call (no LiveKit server here), and a screen reader.
- A picture can be pasted into any picture box, as well as chosen as a file: the profile photo and call background, the Participant and Character sets and the per-space sets on a profile, the environment icon, the sign-in background and the Default and Guest sets on Manage, and a space's picture in its settings. Tab to the picture and press Ctrl+V (Cmd+V on a Mac), or choose the small **Paste** button on its corner (beside the small environment icon), shown on hover and always on a touch screen; the browser may ask to read the clipboard. Enter or Space on the picture still opens the file chooser, and a hint reads "Paste or click to choose". A pasted picture has the same types, the 20 MB limit and the messages of a chosen file; the new messages are "No picture to paste. Copy one first.", "Can't read the clipboard here. Press Ctrl+V now to paste." (focus goes to the picture), on a touch screen "Can't read the clipboard here. Tap the picture to choose one.", and "That picture couldn't be read. Try choosing the file." A box you can't change takes no paste, and a paste in a text field is left alone. Each box has its own name for a screen reader ("Profile picture", "Default Online picture"), in the environment's words. The profile's header now says "Click it to change it, or paste a picture". Chat already took pasted pictures and is unchanged. For page authors: `public/slot-paste.js`, imported by a page, does all of this for every slot, and `tools/check-slot-names.mjs` (now in `npm run check`) holds each slot to a name and each page with slots to the import. No module version changes. Documented in `userguide-images.md` ("Set a picture"), `userguide-accounts.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-spaces.md` and `architecture-overview.md` ("Picture slots"). Verified live in headless Chromium: every kind of slot by Ctrl+V and by the **Paste** button, a text paste, a text field's paste left alone, a 21 MB picture and a BMP refused as chosen files are, the keyboard, a 390 px touch layout, names following the environment's words, and slots that can't be changed. Checked by tools (`tools/check-slot-names.mjs`). Not checked: Firefox, Safari, a real phone, a real screen reader, and a real clipboard holding a file copied from the computer. The **Paste** button gets only a PNG in Chromium; see [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- `POST /api/host/themes/check`, for the host template editor's theme picker (plan-kind-names). A host admin sends a theme file's text (or its JSON), at most 16 KB, from the host console's own origin; the server reads it exactly as a theme import does and stores nothing. 200 `{ theme, dropped }`; 400 with the theme file's sentence; 401 without a host admin's session; 403 from another site; 404 on an environment's own address and on a single install. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` (the host API). Checked by `tools/check-themes.mjs` and `tools/check-origin.mjs`, and verified live with curl on a local server; the template editor's theme picker verified live in headless Chromium.
- An owner can choose what a space opens with the first time someone enters it (GitHub #3, `plan-entering.md` Part 1, steps 1 and 2). Space settings, Space tab, **Opens with**: ticks for the conference (unless it is switched off), the chat and the space's modules, saved as you click, in the order listed (switches since 2026-09-30; see Changed). After a first visit each person's own layout is remembered. With nothing ticked, the page names what opens instead ("Nothing ticked: Chat and To-do open."). A ticked module that can't open now stays listed with a note saying why, such as "(off in this space)". The space stores `opensWith`, up to 20 module ids; `PATCH /api/spaces/:id` takes a list or `null` (owner only; 400 "Opens with must be a list of modules." for anything else), and the space carries it in `GET /api/presence`, `/api/spaces`, `/api/status` and `GET /api/modules/for-space`. A repeated id is dropped and an empty list means not set; the plan kept repeats. Documented in `userguide-spaces.md`, `architecture-overview.md` and `api-modules.md`. Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs` and verified live with curl; the settings verified live in headless Chromium at 1280 and 390 px.
- Joining the call is its own control, in the middle of the space bar: "2 in the call · Join", or **Join** alone with nobody in it. Its hover and focus hint is the microphone note, which has left the space list. It is not shown in the call, in an aside, without the conference permission, with the conference switched off, or on a phone, where the **Conference** tab is the way in. Read as code only; the count and **Join** need a real call. Removed again before release (see Removed).
- A space chooses which pictures the conference shows: Participant images or Character images. The box is the same either way. A space still on the older Roleplaying choice shows Participant images. Both sets stay on the profile page, and Coffee Pub Studio still uses either or both on its own.
- A research link's picture is taken from the page's own structured data when the page gives one: `primaryImageOfPage`, or the picture on the article, product or place. The Open Graph picture is used when the page has none of those, and the old Twitter picture only after that. Checked by `tools/check-link-preview.mjs`.
- A link's menu has **Refresh link**. It reads the page again and updates the title, the description and the picture. Research 0.2.29.
- A new link asks for the address first, then the title, with the page's picture to the right of both. The picture's place is there even before the page is read. Research 0.2.28.
- A research link's card shows the page's picture and its description, so they are there without opening the link. The picture is read by this server. Research 0.2.27.
- Adding a link can read the page. With **Fetch link previews** on for Research, an empty title or description is filled in from the page, and its picture is shown while editing. Off, which is how it starts, the server fetches nothing. Research 0.2.26.
- Entering a space opens the remembered layout, then that space's own list, then the list the environment set for a new space, then the conference (or chat, when the conference is off). A Travel space opens the Planner and chat, and does not open the conference. Home base in that environment opens chat only, because the Planner is not on there. This is step 6 of Planner phases. A space's own list is not stored yet, so the canvas route answers null for it and sends the environment's list beside it; storing it belongs to entering a space (#3). Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs` (`tools/check-travel.mjs` also passes, though it does not test what opens), and the canvas route's answer (`opensWith`, `spaceDefaultsOpensWith`) verified live with curl on 2026-09-30. Not watched in a browser: signing in there was blocked on this machine, and there is no LiveKit server here, so a join never reaches the canvas. A real call was not checked.
- The host admin can edit a bundled template, such as Travel, in place on the host console (GitHub #91, pull request #97; addendum 3 of the templates plan). The id stays and the version goes up, so environments made from it get the words, phases, home icon and module names and icons at once and are offered the rest. The row shows **Edited**; **Reset to shipped** brings the shipped template back and still raises the version; **Duplicate**, on any row, makes a host template at version 1. When a newer image changes a template the host edited, the edits are kept and a notice reads "<name> was updated in this image: review": **Review** compares each part, then **Keep mine** or **Take the new version**. A bundled template can be hidden, never deleted. Stored as `bundledEdits` in `host.json`; new routes `DELETE /api/host/templates/:id/edits`, `POST .../update` and `POST .../duplicate`. Documented in `userguide-templates.md` ("Edit a bundled template") and `architecture-environments.md`; the plan's addendum 3 says built. Verified: the full `npm run check` passed on the Mac, including `tools/check-templates.mjs` and `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`. The console (rows, editor hint, notice, compare, duplicate) was read as code only, not walked live.
- The Planner page shows the plan from the start: the space's name until it is renamed, and a phase line ("Planning · 12 days to go", "Trip · day 3 of 9", or the label alone). The plan form has one optional start and end per phase. An object with no date sits under "Not on a day yet", grouped by phase, and a new one starts in the current phase. A dated phase is named above its first day. Planning starts on the first phase and planning ends at the close of the phase before the main one; the booked start and end stay where they are. Planner 0.10.0. This is step 5 of Planner phases. The plan's 0.8.0 is stale; 0.10.0 is the version after the linked-objects work. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. Verified live in a browser on a throwaway Travel environment: a new trip opened as the space's name with "Planning" and "Nothing on the plan yet. Add something below."; renaming it and setting phase dates showed "Booking · 5 days to go" and a Trip band; clearing the main dates left the name and "Booking"; adding an object with no date put it under "Not on a day yet", in Booking, which is where the phase list started. The home widget still read "No trips planned", so its phase line was not seen. A real call was not checked (there is no LiveKit server here). Planner phases steps 1 to 5 (#95, pull requests #105 to #109) are documented in `userguide-planner.md` ("The plan", "Phases", "Not on a day yet"), `userguide-templates.md` (phases and what a new space opens), `api-module-sdk.md`, `api-modules.md` and `architecture-environments.md`; step 6, what opens on entering, is its own entry above, and the plan now says every step is built. The home widget's phase line was seen live later, on 2026-09-30 ("Booking · 10 days to go", with the trip's dates written through the API rather than the Planner page). The full `npm run check` passed on the Mac.
- A plan stores its phases (`v: 2`): each phase may have a start and an end, the main phase's dates stay the plan's own, and an empty name shows the space's name. The days run from the earliest date to the latest. An object in another phase does not move those dates. An older plan is brought forward once; when the first phase is not the main one, that phase starts on the day the space was created. This is step 4 of Planner phases. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. The pages are the next step. Not verified live in a browser.
- The host template editor lists phases (id, label, one main phase, add, remove, reorder) and what a new space opens, in order. The offer's new-space line names those, in that order. This is step 3 of Planner phases. Verified live in the host console: a new template saved a phase marked main and an ordered open list, Edit showed them back, and a repeated phase id was refused beside the list. The offer sentence was read as code only.
- A module's context answers the space it is open in (`id`, `name`, `createdAt`, or null outside a space) and the template's phases. `host.space()` and `host.phases()` read them. A template edit is on the next read. This is step 2 of Planner phases. Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`. Not verified live in a browser.
- A template can list phases (an id, a label, and at most one main phase) and what a new space opens with. Phases follow the template as it changes. What opens is applied once, with the new-space profile, and a switch offers it when that part changes. The Travel template is version 2: Planning, Booking, Buffer, Pre-trip, Trip, Post-trip, and a new space is set to open the Planner and chat (stored and offered only; nothing opens from it until step 6). This is step 1 of Planner phases. Checked by `tools/check-templates.mjs` and `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`. The host editor does not edit these yet.
- A dated object on a plan is on the Calendar too, and the two stay in step (GitHub #96, pull requests #102 to #104), on top of the finished linked-objects plan (#98 to #101): a link follows its object's title and day unless it is kept on its day, goes when the object is deleted, and a delete that would leave something pointing at nothing says "Used by N." first. Planner 0.9.0, Calendar 1.18.0. Documented in the Planner and Calendar guides, `api-module-sdk.md` ("Keeping links true": `host.objects.onChange`, `holds`, `dated`, `mirror`, `create`, the pairs), `api-modules.md` (`tz`, `refchange`, the backfill) and `architecture-modules.md` ("Keeping links true"); both plans say built, and #96 is off the to-do list. Verified: the full `npm run check` passed on the Mac on the stacked head. Not verified live in a browser. The backfill on a hosted install (once per environment) was read as code only; `tools/check-links.mjs` covers the single-install restart.
- Existing dated objects get their twin once, the first time the server starts after a kind declares that it sends them. A later start leaves that twin as it is. A timed value uses the server's time zone. This is step 3 of keeping plans and the calendar in sync. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`.
- A Planner object with a date, and no pointer, declares that it sends a dated twin. An event declares that it can receive one. Planner 0.9.0, Calendar 1.18.0. This is step 2 of keeping plans and the calendar in sync. The two manifests do not name each other. Read as code only; not verified live in a browser.
- A dated object that sends twins gets one in each kind that receives them, in the same space, and the two stay in step: title, day and time write both ways, clearing the day deletes the twin, and deleting the twin leaves the sender's day until that day changes. A personal object, one that holds a pointer, and a receiver that is off in the space get no twin. A module's own links never remove the pair. This is step 1 of keeping plans and the calendar in sync. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`. Neither module is named.
- Deleting a Calendar event or a Planner object that something points at says "Used by N." before the usual question. Calendar 1.17.18, Planner 0.8.1. This is step 4 of the linked-objects plan. Read as code only.
- The Planner keeps a link on the day you put it (`pinned`, "Keep on this day"). Dragging a link to another day pins it; dragging it back to the object's day clears that. A change to the linked object is picked up through `host.objects.onChange`. Deleting that object removes the link. Planner 0.8.0. This is step 3 of the linked-objects plan. `pinned` is checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. The drag and the live refresh were read as code only.
- A write or delete of a linked object sends `refchange` on the module stream to the modules that point at it (`host.objects.onChange`). A write that leaves the summary the same sends nothing. The writer's time zone travels with the storage write (`tz`); a zone the server does not accept falls back to `TZ`, then UTC. This is step 1 of the linked-objects plan. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`.
- When a module declares `holds` on a kind, deleting the object it points at removes that holder (`onDelete: remove`) or marks it (`onDelete: mark`). A title change rewrites the followed title, and a day change moves a holder that was placed on the old day and is not pinned. The holder is written only when a followed value differs. A bad `holds` is refused. This is step 2 of the linked-objects plan. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs`.
- One input in Chat (#58, phase 1): `/ai` asks the AI privately (thread saved per person), a command picker lists `/ai` and each placed module's commands, and pasting an AI answer with `magpie` blocks (now `objects` blocks; see the formats entry under Changed) offers **Bring in N objects** (a preview with ticks; **Keep ticked**). Research `/r`, To-do `/t`, Planner `/p`, Calendar `/c` and Polls `/v` open that module's add form filled in. The Assistant module is retired; use `/ai` in Chat. Assistant 0.1.20 has no window (`surfaces.canvas.menu: false`, a new manifest field). For module authors: a top-level `commands` field in `module.json`, `host.chat.ask`, `host.ui.moreButton`, and the space's AI, thread, command, actions and objects-check routes (see api-modules, "Chat routes"); threads are kept in `DATA_DIR/ai-threads.json`. Checked by a tool only (`tools/check-one-input.mjs`, `tools/check-modules.mjs`, `tools/check-module-window.mjs` in PRs #77 to #84); not verified live in a browser or a real call. Documented in the new Chat guide.
- Research from any AI can be brought into Magpie as objects (phase 1 of #73, parts 1 and 2). The Assistant has **Copy instructions for another AI** (the text is generated on the server from the same source as the Assistant's own rule) and a **Bring in research** panel: paste a whole answer (`magpie` or `card` fences, or raw JSON), or choose a `.magpie-objects.json` file (now an `objects` block or a `.objects.json` file; see the formats entry under Changed). A preview shows a tick per object; **Keep ticked** saves them through the same Research and Planner actions the Assistant already uses. Imported text ends with the line `External source`. Shared format and checker: `server/object-format.js`, `GET /api/objects/format`, `GET /api/objects/format/schema`, `GET`/`POST /api/modules/:id/objects/check`, and `host.objects.format` / `checkAvailable` / `check`. Part 3 (a direct connection from AI apps) waits for #64. Documented in `userguide-assistant.md` (**Bring in research from another AI**), `userguide-planner.md`, `userguide-research.md`, `architecture-modules.md` ("The objects format"), `api-modules.md` (the routes, the format and every refusal) and `api-module-sdk.md` ("Bringing objects in"); the plan's status says phase 1 is built. Checked by tools on 50ef1bd: `check-object-format`, `check-ai`, `check-assistant` and `check-travel` pass. `npm run check` did not pass on the merge commit (hard-coded words, and the check route missing from the same-origin scan); a follow-up fixed both, and it passes now. The Assistant is 0.1.18.

### Fixed
- Research's window title reads **Research** (or the name the owner gave it), no longer "Research 2" with the number of objects after the name (GitHub #191). Research 0.4.3 (was 0.4.2). Only Research put a count in its title; the count in the header of its page without a titlebar went too. Checked by `tools/check-research.mjs`; read as code by the builder, and passed by quality-assurance.
- With Research off, **Keep** on a note or an event in Chat said "Nothing here can keep that yet." even with the Planner on, which takes both. Keep now finds its place by what modules take. Checked by `tools/check-chat-page.mjs`; verified live by the builder for the preview.
- Fixes from QA of #182 steps 6 and 7 (Calendar 1.23.0, Research 0.3.0, Polls 1.14.0). Items 1 to 3 were verified live by the builder; all five are checked by tool (`check-object-handoff`, `check-research`, `check-travel`, `check-modules`).
  - An event handed to the Calendar whose end is a time with no day of its own, and isn't after the start, now ends the next day (22:00 to 01:00), instead of being given no end.
  - Research's `savePhoto` makes a thumbnail only on the uploader's, an owner's or an admin's page, and otherwise skips it quietly, so the photo shows the whole picture rather than trying and failing; a thumbnail the picture already has is reused.
  - A closed poll offers another module's action only when it can fill every required input: a title, notes, a date (when the winning option has one) and the poll itself.
  - `host.util.detailLines` words a car's `departs` and `arrives` as "Pick-up time" and "Drop-off time", rather than repeating "Pick up" and "Drop off".
  - The bus refuses a `date` or `datetime` input that names a day that doesn't exist (2026-02-31) with 400 and the usual sentence, `<field> must be a date` (or `... a date and time`), where before it was rolled into the next month. `needs` now has both its meanings written down: what a pointed-at object must have, and, on any action, what the request must carry. It was already kept on any action, and the bus still never enforces it.
- A finished poll's **Add it to the calendar** now shows only when the winning option has a date, and puts the poll on the Calendar on that day. Before, it showed on any finished poll and the request was refused for want of a date. Polls 1.14.0, Calendar 1.23.0. Read as code; the Polls form was verified live by the builder, this button not separately.
- A line just before a list in Markdown (a note's "Links:" line, say) is now drawn above the list instead of after it, in Chat, Research and everywhere else `host.util.markdown` draws text. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`.
- Objects dated more than 60 days from the rest of the plan no longer vanish from the Planner. Such an object, saved before, showed on no day and nowhere else; it now shows under **Not on a day yet**, its row reading "Dated <day>, outside the plan", and keeps its date until you change it. Planner 0.12.0. `tools/check-travel.mjs` checks that the page's list includes it by matching the code's text; it was not run in a browser.
- A journey's **Arrival on the ticket** keeps how many days after the departure it lands while the journey is under **Not on a day yet** or on the line, and is dated again from the day it goes back on, instead of losing its day. Planner 0.12.0. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`; the editor's part was not run in a browser.
- Moving a journey to another day (dragging it, **Move to**, changing its day in the editor, or dropping it on a day) now moves its dated **Arrival on the ticket** by the same number of days, rather than leaving the arrival behind on the old date. Planner 0.12.0. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs` for the plan and a drop; the editor's day change is read as code only.
- Opening the editor of an object under **Not on a day yet** and saving no longer moves it to "Before the first day": **When** now shows "Not on a day yet" for it. Planner 0.12.0. Read as code only.
- Asking a module for something from Chat no longer fails with a server error when the input is refused: `POST /api/spaces/:id/action` and `POST /api/spaces/:id/command` answer the refusal's own status and sentence instead of 500, so a command with no text gets 400 "text is needed". A body over 64 KB on these routes and the `/api/bus/` routes gets 413 "that request is over 64 KB". Checked for `/action` and the bus by `tools/check-object-handoff.mjs`; the `/command` case is read as code only.
- Profile's **Other calendars** always says why it can't be used: **Calendar feeds** are off, or the Calendar needs to be approved, turned on, updated or installed in Manage > **Modules**; an owner is told to do it, anyone else that their owner needs to. `GET /api/me/external-calendars` answers `why`. **Calendar feed** now says that Google Calendar reads the address on its own schedule, often every 8 to 24 hours, and Apple Calendar is usually quicker.
- Link previews, Research and chat links now refuse IPv6 literals in addresses (such as `https://[::1]/`), and addresses that resolve to private or special IPv6 ranges, as they did private IPv4 ones.
- Opening a module's page from a space kept its **Back to <space>** link only until the redirect to Calendar or Map: the redirect now keeps the query.
- After **Pop it back in**, the main space bar no longer keeps the pop-out's fold, and Online no longer shows its full words in a narrow pop-out (#160).
- An AI answer's **Keep** shows "Kept" again (#158).
- Map pins sit on their places again, on every Maps surface (a space's canvas, a pop-out, Maps' own page and Map in the top bar). Each pin was drawn lower than its place, further down for each pin before it, because a style gave the pin a position of its own over the one the map library sets (`.pin` in `modules/maps/src/maps.css`). It was already wrong in the previous build. Maps 0.8.0. Read as code and seen by the builder in headless Chromium; the map with WebGL from real tiles was not checked.
- Leaving a space with Maps open no longer logs an error: Maps stops its refresh and its drawing once its markup is gone. Maps 0.8.0.
- A module reminder no longer stops the server. When a module's schedule fired (a Calendar or To-do reminder, say) while anyone it was for had a page open, the whole server stopped and the reminder was lost; a finished map region cut could do the same. It was there before the destinations. Now the schedule timer runs inside its own environment (`moduleHooks.runIn`), every stream listener runs in its own (`listenIn()` in `server/index.js`), a listener that fails is logged and can't stop the server (`tell()` and `safeTick()` in `server/module-hooks.js`), and a notification is stored before anyone is told. Checked by the new `tools/check-hooks.mjs` (4 groups, on a single install and with `BASE_DOMAIN`; it fails on the old code), run again for this entry. Not verified on a real server.
- Saving a place found by the place search now marks the search result as used for anyone who may read Places there, so it is kept when unpicked results are removed. `POST /api/modules/:id/geocode/use` needed the right to change Places at the level of the page, so a result saved into a space from Map in the top bar, by someone who only reads Places for the whole environment, was not marked; it now needs `read`, as the search does. Documented in `api-modules.md`.
- A private conversation no longer tells everyone who is in it. Before, `GET /api/presence` gave anyone signed in, and any guest, where everyone was and every aside's record with its members, private conversations included, and `aside-started` sent a private conversation's members to everyone on the call; the calls cap's refusal named the running space to anyone. Now presence is filtered by membership, a private conversation's `aside-started` carries no members, and the refusal names a space only to its members (see presence by membership under Changed). Checked by `tools/check-presence.mjs`; verified live against the API with a stand-in LiveKit; not tested in a real call.
- Clicking a module card's heading on the spaces page no longer drops the call. While you were in a space, including with the spaces page shown over it, the heading left the page and the call. Now it opens the module's page over the space: you are Away on the call until you go back, and **Back to <space>** returns you. A card's "open in full", a link to a module inside a card, and opening an entry that isn't in a space do the same. When you are in no space, the heading opens the page as before, and Ctrl-, Cmd- or middle-click still opens a new tab. For developers: `openModulePage()` in `public/dashboard.js` sends `app:open-page` with kind `module`, which the space page handles again, deciding with `pageOpens()` in `public/primary-nav.js`. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` and verified in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit; not verified in a real call.
- Going home while you are in a space never drops the call. **Spaces**, the logo and the phone menu's home entry always keep you on the page, also when home is already showing and from the header of a page opened over the call. Before, clicking one of them again on home reloaded the page and dropped the call. Closing a page you opened from home now leaves you on home and still Away, and Away clears when you go back to the space; before, it cleared while you were still on home. For developers: `goHome()` and `HOME_LINKS` in `public/space.js`, and `closeOverlay()` keeps Away while `viewingHome`. Checked by new cases in `tools/check-nav.mjs` (39 tests) and verified in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit at 1280 and 390 pixels: **Spaces** from the space, **Spaces** again, the logo on home, and the logo inside a page opened over home all stayed on the page with the call connected; closing a page opened from home kept Away, and **Back** on the space card cleared it. Not verified in a real call.
- In a space, the space bar's **Modules** list shows again at desktop widths. With the new top bar (#147) the button looked active but no list appeared, so modules couldn't be shown or hidden; a style added for the top bar clipped the space bar's left zone, where the list hangs. The phone's tab bar was not affected. Now only the top bar's own left zone is clipped (`.topbar > .nav-left`). A new case in `tools/check-nav.mjs` fails if any rule clips the space bar or its zones.
- The top bar no longer crowds between 641 and 800 pixels wide. The old bar's right end (the light or dark switch, Manage, the time, your picture) ran over the **Spaces** link there; the new bar drops your name, then the environment's name, and cuts the place name short instead (see the top bar under Changed). Checked in headless Chromium.
- After being pulled into an aside, the space bar shows **Rejoin call** again, and the aside's name reads "<space> · Aside" instead of just "Aside". Before, **Leave** was the only way out, and it took you out of the space. **Rejoin call** takes you back to the space's call, still on it if you were. The cause: the server tells everyone on the call that an aside started (`aside-started`), the people pulled included, and the page listed the aside at once without the space it came from (its `origin`); a pulled person's join found that entry before `GET /api/presence` answered and kept it. The entry is now marked as a stand-in (`pending`), so presence's full record replaces it (`adoptPendingSpace()` in `public/space.js`). Introduced by 54933f7 (the page no longer waits for LiveKit). Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which runs the page's `DataReceived` handler against a stand-in call, then a join and presence's answer, and verified in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit. Pressing **Rejoin call** in a real call is not verified (see TODO.md).
- A person who joined the call after you no longer shows "muted" on their tile while their microphone is on (Thomas's real call, 2026-10-01). The mark now follows their real mute state for everyone, whenever they joined. The cause: the mark was refreshed only on mute and unmute events, and someone joining says they are on the call before their microphone is published, so their tile started marked muted, and a microphone published unmuted sends no unmute event. The mark is now read from the microphone's own state (`micMarkedMuted()` in `public/space.js`) and refreshed whenever a microphone is published, unpublished or subscribed, yours too. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which runs the call's event handlers against a stand-in call (joining, publishing unmuted, muting, unmuting, unpublishing, publishing again, and your own microphone), and verified with a faked LiveKit before and after the fix. Not verified in a real call.
- Away now mutes what you hear as well as your microphone and camera, and nothing opens your microphone until you come back (Thomas's real call, 2026-10-01). Away used to switch off only your own microphone and camera, so you still heard everyone. Now, while you are away, everyone else's audio is muted on your end, including anyone who starts talking after you went away, and the microphone button, **M**, push to talk and joining the call all leave the microphone off; pressing the microphone button or **M** says "away: come back to turn your microphone on" in the status line. Opening your profile or the Manage page over the call, or going to the space list, marks you away as before, so it now mutes what you hear too until you come back; Thomas confirmed this is intended (2026-10-01). **Back** brings back what you heard (unless you had deafened yourself) and the microphone and camera that were on before. Joining the call while away leaves the microphone off, and **Back** turns it on unless you use push to talk. The away button's tooltip now reads "Away: pauses your mic and camera, mutes what you hear, and lets everyone know", and "Back: unpause your mic, camera and sound, and let everyone know" while away. For developers: `hearingOff()` and `applyHearing()` in `public/space.js`. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (what you hear, the microphone button and the join while away) and `tools/check-module-host.mjs` (push to talk opens nothing while away), and verified with a faked LiveKit before and after the fix. Not verified in a real call.
- Someone who joins the call after you went away now sees you as away (found while fixing away, 2026-10-01). Before, going away told only the people on the call at that moment, so anyone who joined, reloaded or reconnected later saw your tile as normal. Now your tile shows "Away", or your away message, for everyone, whenever they arrive. Leaving the call while away clears the mark for everyone. After a dropped connection comes back, others keep your tile, and the server keeps counting you on the call. For developers: away is carried as two participant attributes, `away` (`on` or `off`) and `awayMessage` (`''` for a plain Away), set beside `call` (`awayAttributes()`, `syncAwayAttributes()` and `showAway()` in `public/space.js`); they are said again on connecting and on joining the call, and after a full reconnect `syncCallAttributes()` sends `call` again together with them (before, `call` was not said again, so others might drop your tile), and `stopCall()` clears them with `call` set to `off`. The old one-time `away` data message is still sent and read for one release, for pages still on the old script. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which runs going away and coming back, the reconnect, and the call's event handlers (a late joiner already away, a change of the attributes, the old message), against a stand-in call. Not verified in a real call.
- The camera stays off while you are away (found while fixing away, 2026-10-01). The camera button, **V** and your **Toggle video key** no longer turn it on; they say "away: come back to turn your camera on" in the status line. Changing the camera or the video quality in Settings or your profile while away no longer turns the camera on; the change is applied when you come back, or, if your camera was off, the next time you turn it on (before, turning it on opened the old camera). Going **Back** and then away again quickly no longer leaves your microphone on. For developers: `toggleCam()`, `restartCamera()`, `keepCameraOffWhileAway()` and `keepMicOffWhileAway()` in `public/space.js`. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (the button, a camera change while away applied on **Back** or on the next turn-on, and the microphone after a quick **Back** and away). Not verified in a real call.
- Videos in the pop-out window no longer freeze when the main window is minimised or covered, which happened most in the installed app (Thomas's real call, 2026-10-01). The cause: LiveKit's adaptive stream pauses a video it thinks nobody can see, and it judges that by the main page, so a hidden main window paused every video, and it makes an exception for the browser's own picture-in-picture but not for this pop-out window. While a video from someone else is in the pop-out, Collaborator now tells LiveKit it is shown in a window of its own, at its size there; popping back in drops that. For developers: `keepPoppedVideoLive()`, `keepPoppedVideosLive()` and `dropPoppedWatches()` in `public/space.js`, which rely on LiveKit's internal `observeElementInfo()` (see TODO.md). Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, and verified with a faked LiveKit before and after the fix. Not verified in a real call or in the installed app.
- The space bar's Modules list and the who's here list now open above a module floating or snapped on the canvas, instead of underneath it (also in the pop-out window); on a phone, an open who's here list no longer shows through your profile page. The cause was the header's stacking order: the header is its own layer (20), under the modules (25), so the lists could not draw above a module however high their own z-index (60). The lists now share the account menu's level, a `--z-menu` token (26, above the module layer and under a page opened over the call) in `public/style.css`, and while either list is open the header rises to it. Guarded by `tools/check-nav.mjs`. Verified live in headless Chromium before and after the fix (floating, snapped and docked modules at 1280, 900 and 720 pixels wide, the pop-out window, and the profile page over the call at 1280 and 390); checked by a tool. Not checked in a real call.
- A join cancelled while the browser was still getting the microphone no longer publishes it. Hanging up, switching the Conference off or losing the connection while a join was still under way could leave your microphone published, and you shown as on the call, with the Conference gone. Each step of a join now checks whether it was stopped meanwhile, and backs out, releasing the microphone. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit; not checked with a real microphone in a real call.
- Push to talk does nothing when you are not on the call. Holding the key out of the call could open and publish your microphone while you were only in the space. Letting go still always stops talking. Checked by `tools/check-module-host.mjs`.
- With push to talk on and its key at Space (the default), Space presses a button, menu entry or other control you reached with the keyboard in a space, instead of talking. Since the start (b37b0a1) push to talk took Space everywhere outside a text field. Now Space or Enter presses a control reached by Tab or the arrow keys, and picks an entry in an open menu. On the page itself, on a link, or on a button you just clicked with the mouse (including after picking from a menu with the mouse, and after clicking a button you had first reached by keyboard) the key still talks, so clicking the microphone and then holding Space talks. Typing in a field is unchanged. With another push-to-talk key, such as T, Space always presses controls; with Enter as the key, Enter on a link talks rather than following it. Fixed in `public/space.js` (`pressesControl`, which tracks whether focus arrived by keyboard or by pointer), in the space and its pop-out windows; no module version changes. Documented in `userguide-call.md` ("Push to talk"); the entry is off [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md). Verified live in headless Chromium, with the microphone call spied on and "in a space" set by hand (no LiveKit server here); guarded by `tools/check-module-host.mjs` (35 groups). Not checked: a real call, the pop-out windows (read as code only; the check pins that they are wired), Firefox and Safari. Still open: the SDK menu's link entries don't open on Space ([TODO](documentation/TODO.md)).
- Push to talk no longer leaves the microphone open. Since the start (b37b0a1) talking could carry on after you let go of the key if focus had moved into a text field meanwhile, if a modifier such as Shift came down before you let go, or if you switched window or tab, or left the page, while holding it. Letting go of the key that started talking now always stops it, and so does switching window or tab, hiding the tab or leaving the page. Fixed in `public/space.js` (`releaseTalk`, and the key's code kept while it is held), in the space and its pop-out windows. Documented in `userguide-call.md` ("Push to talk"). Verified live in headless Chromium with the microphone call spied on; the window switch and the hidden tab were fired by hand, not done for real. Guarded by `tools/check-module-host.mjs`. Not checked: a real call, real window switching, the pop-out windows (read as code only), Firefox and Safari.
- A guest's header, and a guest's menu on a phone, no longer end with a divider before nothing. A tool that other code hides, such as your picture for a guest, now counts as not shown, and the bar is drawn again when it is hidden or shown (`isShown()` and a watch on `hidden` in `public/nav-bar.js`). Verified live in headless Chromium on a guest's page (the pages were checked at 390, 640, 641 and 1280 pixels wide); checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs`.
- The header's menu on a phone works from the keyboard. Opening it puts focus on its first entry; Tab moves through the entries and back to the menu button; Escape closes it and puts focus back on the button; Tabbing out of the menu or its button closes it. Widening the window past the phone width closes the menu, and an account menu open when the window narrows to a phone closes too, with focus left on something you can see. Verified live in headless Chromium at 390, 640, 641 and 1280 pixels wide. Not checked with a real screen reader.
- Moving **Grid size** in the space bar no longer makes snapped modules overlap. Each snapped module moves to the cells nearest where it was put and keeps about its size on screen, not its number of cells. No two overlap while the canvas can hold them all at their smallest size: if rounding to whole cells leaves no room, they all shrink a little together, so equal modules stay equal; then they go in rows at their smallest size; they share cells only when even that does not fit. Moving the slider back gives the old sizes and places back exactly. While you drag, the modules already sit where letting go leaves them, and the layout is saved once, when you let go (each arrow key press counts as letting go). It stays fast, a few milliseconds a step, and a crowded canvas no longer freezes. The grid's arithmetic moved to `public/snap-grid.js` (`resettle`), and each module remembers the box it was put in (`placed`). Documented in `architecture-canvas.md` ("Free or snapped") and `userguide-call.md`. Verified live in headless Chromium: four modules from 130 to 50 to 320 and back to 130, by keyboard and by mouse, never overlapping and back in identical boxes; five and six modules sharing only where they can't fit; a drag followed by a size change; a module snapped on its own; the snap switch off and on; and an older saved layout without `placed`. Checked by tools: `tools/check-canvas.mjs` runs the real snap-all tilings of 2 to 8 modules on several canvas sizes at every grid size, checks that equal boxes stay equal, and holds the arithmetic to time budgets; QA also ran 3,000 random layouts. Not checked: a real call (no LiveKit server here) or a phone with touch. Two older problems remain, after a reload and after a window resize; see [known-issues](documentation/known-issues.md).
- On a phone, the header's menu no longer runs 16 pixels past the right edge of the screen. Verified live in headless Chromium at 390 pixels wide.
- A page opened over a call keeps its way back. Going from your profile to Manage, or from Manage to **View profile**, kept the page over the call but lost its **Back to** button. **Sign out** on such a page now signs the whole window out, not only the page over the call. Verified live in headless Chromium, except over a real call (no LiveKit server here); read as code there.
- **Clear** in a module's own window works again (the title bar's **…**, **Clear**, then "Clear everything and start again?"). Since the Clear menu came in (2aaf316) it stopped with "scope is not defined" and cleared nothing. It now clears that module's data in the space the window is open in, or in the environment when the window is not in a space. A role without the right still gets "your role can't do that in this module". Fixed in `public/module.js`; no module version changes. Guarded by `tools/check-module-host.mjs`, and verified live: an admin in a space, an admin with no space, a guest refused, and a module kept for the environment only.
- An install whose environment was never renamed now takes the product's name when `PRODUCT_NAME` is set but empty or only spaces. It kept the old placeholder name, "Coffee Pub Tavern". `PRODUCT_NAME` unset, empty or blank now means the default, Collaborator, everywhere, by one rule (`productName()` in `server/product-name.js`, where the default is written once). Documented in `architecture-environments.md`. Checked by `tools/check-templates.mjs`, which starts servers with `PRODUCT_NAME` set to `""` and `"   "`, and verified live: a single install with each value answered "Collaborator" for the product name and the environment's name in `/api/branding` and started with "Collaborator v0.4.0"; a hosted server with `" "` answered "Collaborator" in `/api/product`.
- A space's **Turn AI off in this space** is kept after a restart. Since the switch was added (b37b0a1, PR #78) it was dropped every time the data file was loaded, so AI came back on after a restart and the next save erased the setting. Checked by `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`, which restarts a server and confirms the setting is kept and AI still refused, and verified live.
- The Chat, Assistant, Themes and Templates guides name the files as the server writes and reads them: now `.objects.json`, `.theme.json` and `.template.json` (they said `.magpie-`), and the Assistant guide calls the pasted blocks `objects` blocks. Checked against `server/object-format.js`, `server/theme-file.js` and `server/template-file.js`.
- The help for Research's **Fetch link previews** setting no longer calls a link a card. It now reads "The link shows the picture and that description.", which also makes the names check pass again after PR #137. Research 0.2.30. Checked by tools (the names check and the other checks) and seen served by a running server; not viewed in a browser.
- Portrait size is a conference setting, under Call features. The conference tile and Coffee Pub Studio's participant box both use it. Stream does not keep its own. Stream 0.1.7.
- A conference portrait uses the Online picture in the same order as the other views: this space's own, then that person's, then the default set. The profile photo is used only when none of those exist.
- A conference portrait with a background is a centered picture over that background, the same size when the camera is off and when that person is away. It is not stretched to fill the tile.
- A planner card's note is drawn with the same markdown as chat and research, and a link in it opens in a new window. Planner 0.10.6.
- A note kept from chat takes its type from the imported icon, or from its kind when the icon is a general one: a flight is a plane, a hotel stays a hotel, a meal is a meal. Research 0.2.25.
- In card view a research note, link or answer shows its whole text, so a long one is taller than a short one. The list still shows one line. Research 0.2.24.
- A research note, link or answer is drawn with the same markdown as chat, so bold, lists and links show as such. The note editor has the same Bold, Italic, Code and list buttons. Research 0.2.23.
- Chat in grid mode fills its window: the messages use the space, and the input sits across the bottom. Docked, it was already fine.
- A research note has a type, chosen from icons: a general note, a flight, a hotel, a map, and others. The title sits beside that icon. Research 0.2.22.
- An import in chat is a message titled **Imported**, with the same header as any other message: portrait, time, private or public, and the menu.
- Turning the grid on keeps a layout the user already placed, each window on the cells nearest where it is, clear of the others. Windows that were never placed are tiled across the canvas instead of stacked. Dragging or resizing one remembers that place for the next time the grid is on.
- Only the person who posted a chat message can change it between private and public. Everyone else still sees which it is.
- A module's buttons stay inside the module. They are icons, and as the module runs out of room they fold into **...** from the left, the same way the conference bar does. Bring in research uses icon buttons and folds the same way. Calendar 1.18.2, To-do 1.11.17, Polls 1.12.19, Planner 0.10.5. Research's bar already had icons; the host now draws the icon alone.
- Opening Bring in research on an empty chat no longer squashes the formatting row, and closing it brings that row back. The import panel shrinks and scrolls instead of pushing the row out of the column.
- An AI question and its answer keep the time they were written. Opening the space was painting every saved turn as now, because the stored time was ignored.
- A command for a module that is not open asks what to do: add it and open the module, add it and leave the module closed, or cancel.
- A chat message shows its date and its time.
- Chat scrolls again. A long thread was growing the column and the bottom was cut off, including the object cards. A sender's portrait is their photo.
- Chat's **Save the chat**, **Delete the chat** and **Clear your AI thread** are in the chat header's **…** menu, under that button. **Delete the chat** is for an owner or a moderator. It removes every shared message for everyone, and every private AI thread in that space, so the chat stays empty after a refresh. An owner or a moderator can delete any one message. Anyone else can delete only a message they sent, including a question or answer in their own AI thread. That delete stays gone after a refresh. **Clear your AI thread** still leaves answers already shared in the chat.
- The title bar **…** menu opens under that button. A docked module has no box of its own, so the menu was measured from the left edge of the canvas and opened over the chat. It is now placed from the button, the same way a menu inside a module is.
- Clear sits in the host's own **…** menu, under that button, on every installed module on a space (and in a module's own window). The conference and the chat do not have it. **Clear**, then **Clear everything and start again?**, deletes everything that module holds in that space. A dated object's twin goes with it. The Planner no longer draws its own clear menu. Planner 0.10.4. Checked by `tools/check-links.mjs` and `tools/check-travel.mjs`. Not watched in a browser.
- An edit window in a module stays open when you click outside it or drag a selection past its edge. Save, Cancel or Close is what dismisses it. The Planner, Calendar, To-do, Polls, Places and Research editors all did this. Calendar 1.18.1, To-do 1.11.16, Polls 1.12.18, Places 0.8.19 and Research 0.2.21.
- The old product name, Magpie, no longer appears in anything a person reads (#110). Those sentences use `PRODUCT_NAME`, which defaults to Collaborator. Theme, template and research files that still use the old keys or file names are refused. The formats were first renamed after the current product; they are now named by their kind instead, and files and answers named after either product are refused (see "Theme, template and research files ... are named by what they are" under Changed). `tools/check-names.mjs` fails if a product name is written back into the code (see the product name guard under Changed). Planner 0.10.2 and To-do 1.11.15 are comment-only. Checked by `tools/check-themes.mjs`, `tools/check-templates.mjs`, `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`, `tools/check-object-format.mjs`, `tools/check-modules.mjs` and `tools/check-names.mjs`. Not watched in a browser. The guides, the architecture and API documents and the design system no longer say the old name, apart from the container names and data paths an old install still has; several plans still do. The logo images still read it (see known-issues).
- A Planner trip no longer grows past 60 days, which is as long as the plan shows. A day that does not fit is left off, and the page says so. Planner 0.10.1. Fixes #76. Checked by `tools/check-travel.mjs`. The note on the page was read as code only.
- The call page and the space list no longer sit still for 10 seconds when LiveKit does not answer (GitHub #71, pull request #93). The server gives up on the call service after 2 seconds, and `GET /api/presence` and `GET /api/status` then answer with nobody in a call. Joining a space, a reload back into one and the join screen no longer wait on presence; it fills in when it answers and tries again on the next poll. Documented in `architecture-overview.md` and `architecture-environments.md`. Verified: checked by a tool on a server pointed at an address that does not answer (`/api/presence` in 2017 ms, `/api/status` in 2046 ms, both 200; the case is in `tools/check-host-registry.mjs`), and the full `npm run check` passed on the Mac. The page's give-up was read as code only. Not verified on the real server or in a real call.
- A server with environments that was started without `BASE_DOMAIN` wrote a second, empty install into its data folder, and every later start with `BASE_DOMAIN` then refused (GitHub #90, PR #92). Now a start without `BASE_DOMAIN` on a data folder with `host.json` or `environments/` stops with "This data folder belongs to a server with environments: set BASE_DOMAIN." and writes nothing. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` and `userguide-getting-started.md`. Checked by tools: `npm run check` passed on the PR branch, including the new case in `tools/check-host-registry.mjs`. Not verified on the real server.

### Changed
- **The space bar on a phone** (GitHub #160, `plan-phone-space-bar.md`). On a phone, a narrow window or a pop-out 640 pixels or narrower, it reads: the module tabs that fit (at least 56 pixels each, the shown one always kept), **Join**, Online's count, **Rejoin** in an aside, **…** (**More**, only when something is in it) and **Leave**, always inside the window. **…** holds the tabs that didn't fit, a line, then **Pull participants back** and the modules' tools; in a narrow pop-out also **Full screen** ("Exit full screen" while on) and **Pop it back in**. Its badge adds up what it holds, and if the Conference's tab is in it while you are on the call it shows the green dot, red while your microphone is live. Join no longer squeezes the tabs: one tab fewer shows. A narrow pop-out shows as many tabs as the main window would, though it always needs **…** for Full screen and Pop out; its bar sits on the header's second row, sliding away with it when idle, and Online shows its count. Online's list opens upward on a phone.
  - **For module authors:** `host.nav.set` is unchanged; on a phone a module's tools are always entries in **…**. For page code: registry fields `phone: 'bar' | 'popout'`, `short` and `activeLabel`; `nav` exports `phoneTabs`, `phoneFold` and `TAB_MIN`. Documented in `architecture-canvas.md` ("Phones"), `architecture-navigation.md`, `api-module-sdk.md` and `userguide-call.md`. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (63 tests), run again for this entry; not verified on a real phone.
- A window holds only as many docked modules as it has columns for (GitHub #156, `plan-docked-limit.md`). Every docked column is at least 240 pixels wide (it was 160): 2 columns from 641 pixels, 3 from 760, 4 from 1000, 5 from 1280, 6 from 1600, 7 from 1920. The Conference and the Chat always keep their columns; installed modules give way from the right. A module with no column floats and waits: its remembered mode stays docked, and it docks again in its old place, at its width, when the window is wide enough; dragging or resizing it makes it floating for good. Its **Dock** is dimmed ("Docks when the window is wide enough"); another floating module's says "The window is too narrow for another column" while the columns are full; the Conference's and the Chat's always works, and the rightmost module waits instead. **Layout ▾** says why at the end of **Arrange** ("The window is too narrow for another column, so Calendar is floating. It docks when the window is wide enough.", or after **Dock all** "N docked. M stay floating until the window is wide enough."), clearing after 6 seconds or when the panel closes; nothing is said on entering, resizing or popping out. 640 pixels and below is the phone layout for both the space bar and the canvas, where nothing waits. Floating, modules in their own window and phones are unchanged.
  - For developers: `public/snap-grid.js` exports `DOCK_MIN`, `FLEX_MIN`, `NARROW`, `dockLimit()` and `fitDock()`; `createCanvas()` takes `onNote`; `dockAll()` returns `{ docked, waiting }`. Documented in `architecture-canvas.md`, `architecture-navigation.md`, `userguide-call.md` and `userguide-modules.md`. Checked by new cases in `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, run again for this entry; not verified in a real call.
- New environments start with Thomas's curated reactions and icons (GitHub #169): 105 reactions, whose first six, the **1** to **6** keys, are Heart, Black Heart, 100%, Hi, Thumbs up and Thumbs down, and 113 icons (111 solid, plus the brands `bluesky` and `xbox`). An existing environment still on exactly the old six reactions or the old 21 icons moves to the new list once; reactions and icons are judged separately, and a list an owner changed is kept. A template may now carry up to 120 reactions and 120 icons (it was 30 and 60); a template's `iconSet` still takes only solid icons. For developers: the lists are in `server/default-lists.js`; the settings flags `reactionsCuratedSeeded` and `iconsCuratedSeeded` record the one-time move; `MAX_REACTIONS` and `MAX_ICON_SET` in `server/templates.js`. Documented in `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-call.md` and `userguide-templates.md`. Checked by the new `tools/check-defaults.mjs` (9 cases), run again for this entry; not verified on a real server.
- **Chat keeps one kind of message, private or public** (GitHub #157, `plan-chat-model.md`). A command you type, `/ai` included, shows as your own private message with its module's icon and colour; an AI answer is a private message from **AI**, in gold. Click a message's **private** or **public** badge to **Make public** or **Make private**: the message itself moves, with no copy, and appears for or disappears from everyone else at once. A public AI answer reads **AI, for <name>** with the question above it. A filter above the messages, **All | Private | Public**, hides the rest on your screen. **Delete your private messages** in Chat's menu removes all of yours in the space. **Shared**, **Share to the space**, "AI answer shared by …" and **Clear your AI thread** are gone. Each module has a colour, shown on its messages, its titlebar icon and its line in **Layout ▾**: AI gold, Research blue, To-do green, Calendar red, the Planner teal, Polls purple, Places orange, Maps pink. The Assistant's **Use** permission no longer decides who may use `/ai`; **Turn AI off in this space** still does. Calendar 1.19.3, Maps 0.8.1, Places 0.9.1, Polls 1.12.21, Research 0.2.33, To-do 1.12.3, Planner 0.11.2.
  - Storage: public messages stay in `chat.json`, in its old shape, and private ones go in a new `chat-private.json` beside it, so going back to an earlier release keeps private messages private. A flip moves a message between the two, destination first; one found in both counts as private. Messages carry new optional fields `visibility`, `kind`, `command`, `module`, `summaries` and `replyTo`, and a read-only `question` on a public AI answer. A space keeps 500 public messages, and each person 200 private ones, 30 days. On the first start the old `ai-threads.json` is moved into `chat-private.json` once and renamed `ai-threads.moved.json`; a previously shared answer stays private, since its copy is already in the chat. Deleting the chat, deleting your private messages and deleting a space reach both files.
  - Routes: new `PATCH /api/spaces/:id/chat/:messageId` `{ visibility }` (the author only; 400, 403, 404, 429) and `DELETE /api/spaces/:id/chat/private`; the call hears `chat-visibility`. `POST /api/spaces/:id/command` and `/ai` return the stored private `message` (and `question`); `share` is ignored. `/ai/thread` keeps its old shape, read from the chat. `GET /api/modules/for-space` gives each module's `color`.
  - **For module authors:** a manifest may set `color` to one of gold, blue, green, teal, purple, red, orange or pink; any other value refuses the install. Module pages get the eight `--tint-*` tokens from `/sdk/host.css` and with the theme. New: `data-mode-shown` on the page, so the light tints show under the environment's light default.
  - Documented in `api-modules.md` ("Chat routes", the manifest), `api-module-sdk.md` ("Theme"), `architecture-overview.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `design-theme.md` ("Tints"), `userguide-chat.md`; `plan-chat-model.md` is built and `plan-one-input.md` notes its steps 7 and 8 are superseded. Checked by the new `tools/check-chat-model.mjs` (28 checks, the move from AI threads included) and `tools/check-chat-page.mjs` (10 tests), run again for this entry; quality assurance's own runs are theirs. Not verified in a real call.
- **Join the call** is in the space bar (GitHub #165): **Layout ▾ | Join the call | Online**. One press shows the Conference if it is hidden and joins; it is gone while you are on the call, and in an aside it joins the aside's call. On a phone it is a **Join** tab with a green phone. The label is fixed, with no verb to change it. This is a deliberate exception to `plan-entering.md` change 3, which had taken the call control out of the space bar; entering a space still never joins the call. Documented in `userguide-call.md` and `architecture-navigation.md`.
- The space bar's **Modules ▾** button is now **Layout ▾**, with a new icon, and it holds the canvas's layout too (`plan-layout-menu.md`, steps 1 and 2; Thomas, 2026-10-02). Its panel has two sections. **Show** is the module switches, as before. **Arrange** holds **Dock all** ("Dock every floating <module> beside the call"), **Clean up** ("Tidy the floating <modules>"), **Snap to a grid**, a switch, and **Grid size**, a slider shown only while snap is on. **Clean up** moves the floating modules fully onto the canvas so none overlap, keeping each one's size where it fits; with snap on each keeps its size in cells wherever a place exists, and only one with no place is made smaller. Pressing it again changes nothing. It leaves docked modules and modules in their own window alone, and the new places are remembered. Dock all, snap and Grid size are gone from the space bar's right side and its **…**, which now hold Full screen, Pop out, Pull participants back or Rejoin call in an aside, a module's own tools and Leave space. Phones are unchanged: no Layout button, the modules are tabs. No new shortcut and no SDK change.
  - The button's word is a verb an owner or a template can change: the verbs are now `enter` and `layout` (default "Layout"), and Manage > Template > **Words** has the row "Button for the {canvas} layout". The template's and owner's `verbs` take `layout`; the refusals name both verbs.
  - For developers: the button keeps its id `#modules-toggle` and the controls theirs (`#dock-all`, `#snap-all`, `#snap-size`, new `#clean-up`); the panel's sections are `#modules-menu-show` and `#modules-menu-arrange`, and `update()` in `public/canvas.js` rewrites only Show; `canvas.cleanUp()` uses the new pure `tidyCells()` in `public/snap-grid.js` for snapped modules (`resettle()` only when they can't all fit even at their smallest) and the new `tidyBoxes()` for free ones.
- The top bar's links share one style: Calendar, Map, **Spaces ▾** and "› <space>" are all `core-link` at 13px (Spaces was 14px and the space's segment 15px). `tools/check-nav.mjs` holds it.
  - Both documented in `architecture-navigation.md`, `architecture-canvas.md`, `architecture-environments.md` and `architecture-overview.md` (the verbs), `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-modules.md`, `userguide-environment-settings.md`, `userguide-calendar.md` and `userguide-todo.md`. Checked by tools, each run again for this entry: `tools/check-nav.mjs` (56 tests) and `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (including `tidyCells` and `tidyBoxes`: no overlaps, every box inside, a box that fits keeps its size). Quality assurance passed the batch; not verified in a real call.
- The header is two bars of two zones each, with nothing centred (`plan-two-zone-nav.md`, steps 1 to 5; Thomas, 2026-10-02: "we have overcomplicated the nav layout").
  - **The top bar** reads, left to right: the logo and the environment's name, **Calendar** and **Maps** when shown, **Spaces ▾**, and only in a space "› <space>" (in an aside "› <space> › Aside: <names>"). No other page adds anything there: Manage, a profile or a module's page no longer show "› Manage" or their name. From 641 to 820 pixels Calendar and Maps are icons only. The right side, the bell and your picture, is unchanged.
  - **Every other page has a second bar**, in the space bar's colour: its name and then its controls. Manage and Profile have their tabs there; someone's profile and a space's settings have **Manage** first (owners and the admin only), then the name and the tabs; AI configuration and a module's configuration have **Manage** and the name; the module settings page and a module's page have the name. Calendar and Map keep their bar with no name, and their entry in the top bar is marked. On a phone the top bar shows the page's name as a plain label, and the second bar holds only the controls.
  - **The space bar** is two zones in one colour: **Modules**, then **Online** (who is here, unchanged) on the left, and the right side unchanged. As it narrows, Online gives way first, then the right side folds into **…**. The phone's tab bar is unchanged.
  - **Themes** have three top bar colour areas: the branding area, the left (the header background) and the right side. Manage > Theme has four new rows, **Branding area background** and **text**, and **Right side background** and **text**, each on **Auto** until set, which looks as before. They are new optional keys `navBrandBg`, `navBrandText`, `navRightBg` and `navRightText` (`--nav-brand-bg`, `--nav-brand-text`, `--nav-right-bg`, `--nav-right-text`), `null` or missing for Auto; theme files export and import them, and old themes and files load unchanged. `--nav-primary-edge-bg` is now opaque, with the same look.
  - For developers: the registry has two zones, `left` and `right`, and `register()` refuses `middle`; `phoneZones()` returns `{ left, bar, right }`; `fitWidth()` replaces `middleWidth()` and `middleCentred()`; `#subnav-middle` is gone; `renderPageBar()` and `pageAnchor()` are new and `crumbLink()` is removed. Documented in `architecture-navigation.md`, `design-theme.md`, `architecture-overview.md` ("Themes"), `api-module-sdk.md`, `userguide-spaces.md`, `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-themes.md` and `userguide-environment-settings.md`.
  - **For module authors:** `zone` in `host.nav.set` is ignored from this release: every module tool goes in the space bar's right zone, where it can fold. A tool that names `left` or `middle` is placed there, not refused. No bundled module is affected.
  - Checked by tools, each run again for this entry: `tools/check-nav.mjs` (54 tests), `tools/check-theme.mjs` (14 checks) and `tools/check-themes.mjs` (18 groups). Not checked here in a browser by me, not in a real call, and not with a screen reader, Firefox, Safari or a real phone.
- Research's **Fetch link previews** now starts on (Thomas, 2026-10-02). In an environment that never changed the setting, adding a link fills in an empty title or description from that page and shows its picture; the request goes from this server to the address typed. An owner can turn it off in Research's settings (**Module Configuration**), and an environment that turned it off, or turns it off now, stays off. The protections are unchanged: a private, loopback or link-local address is refused, after a redirect too. Research 0.2.32 (the setting's `default` in `module.json` is now `true`). Documented in `userguide-research.md`. Checked by tools: `tools/check-link-preview.mjs` (9 checks, run again for this entry) has a new case that holds the default on, a saved off staying off, and staying off after a restart. Not verified live in a browser or against a real page here.
- Every switch shows an icon beside its word: the chat's **Private | Shared**, the Calendar's **Month | Week | Day | Agenda**, the Planner's **Days | Decisions | Bookings | Money**, **Open | Closed | All** in Polls and **Open | Done | All** in the To-do, **Mine | This space** in Places and Research, and on the Calendar page **Month | Week | Day** and **Agenda | To-do**. Too narrow for the words, a switch shows its icons only; hovering shows the word, and a screen reader reads it. The To-do's **Open | Done | All** on the Calendar page now looks like every other switch. The chat's hint shortens to "Chat, or / for commands" or "Chat" in a narrow chat, which also stops it wrapping after the larger **Send** button. Polls 1.12.20, To-do 1.12.2, Planner 0.11.1; Calendar stays 1.19.2.
  - **For module authors:** `host.ui.viewSwitch` options take `icon` (and `regular`); a new `element` option draws the switch in the module's own page. The look is in `/sdk/host.css` (`.tb-tabs` and its parts), which a module must not size; a new check, `tools/check-switches.mjs` (8 cases, passing when run again for this entry), holds that and that every switch is drawn the shared way with its icons. No new tokens. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` and `design-theme.md` ("The switch"). Read as code for the narrow widths; not looked at in a browser here.
- The Calendar's views are **Month | Week | Day | Agenda** (Calendar 1.19.2). **List** is now **Agenda**, and **Month + list** is gone: Month and Week always list their events under the grid, and Day doesn't. **Open on** offers Month, Week, Day and Agenda, and a saved Month + list opens on Month; nothing stored changes. Calendar in the top bar keeps **Month | Week | Day** with nothing listed under the grid, since its **Agenda** panel is the list, so on a phone the month's list is the **Agenda** tab. Documented in `userguide-calendar.md`.
- While Calendar is in the top bar, the To-do card's heading on home opens Calendar on its **To-do** panel (the **To-do** tab on a phone), over a space or in a new tab too, and that counts as your choice: the Calendar card's heading then opens on whichever panel you chose last. For developers: `GET /api/modules/widgets` gives a destination's panel module the `href` `<path>#panel=<module id>` (`/calendar#panel=todo`), and the destination page reads `#panel=` (`hashPanel()` in `public/primary-nav.js`), then takes it out of the address. Documented in `api-modules.md`, `architecture-navigation.md` and `userguide-todo.md`. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (49 tests) and `tools/check-destinations.mjs` (35 groups), run again for this entry; read as code for the page.
- The add buttons of the Calendar, the To-do, Polls and the Planner, Places' **+** and the chat's **Send** have icons as large as the call's controls, a 20px icon in a 38px button, keeping that share at the smaller sizes and on a phone. A module's bar gets the same square icon buttons when it floats or is on its own page as when it is docked. **For module authors:** the base stylesheet (`/sdk/host.css`) has `.action-btn`, with `.primary` for the accent look; its size comes from `--action-btn-h` and `--action-icon-ratio`, which a module must not set. A new check, `tools/check-buttons.mjs` (in `npm run check`), keeps the button defined once in `public/style.css` and `public/sdk/host.css` and fails a bundled module that sizes it. Research 0.2.31 drops its own now-unused button styles. Documented in `design-theme.md` ("The action button") and `api-module-sdk.md` ("The action bar"). `check-buttons` passes, run again for this entry; the sizes were not looked at here in a browser.
- Home's **Coming up** and **Due soon** cards open Calendar in the top bar while it is shown. An event or a task opens there with its editor open (a task on the **To-do** panel, or its tab on a phone), and a day in the small month opens Calendar at that day; from inside a space it opens over the space and you are Away on the call. Without Calendar shown they work as before, and **Need your vote** is unchanged. Ctrl-, Cmd- or Shift-click, a middle click, or Ctrl or Cmd with Enter opens a new tab and leaves home and the call alone. The Planner's **Trips** card is gone from home; a summary of each space's plan on its space card is planned (`plan-dashboard.md`, phase 5). Calendar 1.19.1, To-do 1.12.1, Planner 0.11.0.
  - **For module authors:** `host.objects.open(ref, { newTab })` and `host.page.open(hash, { newTab })`. On home, a widget's own objects and `page.open` go to the destination its module is part of while that is shown (`tileRefTarget()` in `public/primary-nav.js`). Documented in `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-navigation.md`, `architecture-modules.md` and the Calendar, To-do, Planner and Spaces guides. Checked by new cases in `tools/check-nav.mjs` (48 tests) and `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` (50 groups), both run again for this entry; read as code for the rest, not verified in a real call.
- Calendar 1.19.0, To-do 1.12.0, Maps 0.8.0 and Places 0.9.0, for Calendar and Map in the top bar.
  - **Calendar** has a **Day** view, on its own page and in a space as well (and in **Open on**, which also sets Calendar in the top bar's first view on a wider screen). On the environment's calendar a space's events can be changed where you may add events there, and others open read-only, saying "Only people who can add events in <space> can change this."; a new event asks **Where** (the environment's own calendar, named by the environment's name, or a space where you may add) every time, and **Save** says "Pick a <space> first." until you choose. A space's event reminds that space's people. Anyone may drop on a day now; what is offered is only what they may do. It offers the destination's calendar (`main`) and its **Agenda** (`panel`).
  - **To-do** works the same way on its environment page (**Where**, editing a space's tasks where you may, reminders in the task's space) and offers the **To-do** panel: open tasks grouped **Overdue**, **Today**, **This week**, **Later** and **No date**, with **Add task** and **Open | Done | All** at its top.
  - **Maps** gains the `environment` scope and an environment page, offers Map's main part (the filter, dimming, the search's pins, the shared selection, **+** at the map's top left), and reads only objects with a position, up to 1,000, so a busy space's pins are all drawn rather than the newest 50.
  - **Places** offers Map's panel: Mine, the environment's own and each space's places in one list, each marked with where it is kept, the category chips counting what the filter and search leave; **Where** in the dialog for a new place, its prompt naming only what is offered ("Pick where it goes first." until one is picked), and for a read-only place showing its place, greyed; the place menu's copies name their target ("Save to mine", "Share to <space>"); one search for the list and the map.
  - Modules are told the environment's name (`info.context.environment.name`), and the Calendar, the To-do and Places name the environment's own list by it.
- **For module authors:** what the SDK gained for destinations, all documented in `api-module-sdk.md`.
