  - On a module's environment page, `{ space: <id> }` on `host.storage.get`, `set`, `delete` and `list`, `host.schedule`, `host.cancelSchedule`, `host.notify` (`to: 'space'` is then that space) and `host.objects.setLinks` reads and writes one of the viewer's spaces; the server checks the module's permission there. `host.spaces()` (and `GET /api/modules/:id/spaces-data`) gives each space `write`. A page in a space still reaches only its own.
  - `host.objects.search(text, { scope: 'spaces', has: 'place' })`: every space the viewer is a member of at once, each pointer with its space, and only summaries with a place, up to 1,000 (`GET /api/objects/search?scope=spaces&has=place`). Environment pages only.
  - `host.actions.list({ ref })` lists only the actions the viewer could carry out where that object is, and the drop menu passes the dropped pointer. From an environment page, a request about one of the provider's own objects in a space is checked by the viewer's right to change the provider in that space, and only someone with that right may take it (`GET /api/bus/actions?ref=`, `space` on a request, `pending`, `claim` and `complete` checked there).
  - `info.context.environment.name`, and `info.context.destination` with `host.destination` on a destination's part.
  - `host.presence`'s people carry `asidePrivate` (from step 3 of `plan-primary-nav.md`).
- Where people are is shown only for the spaces you belong to (`plan-primary-nav.md` step 3, decision 6). Everyone belongs to the Lobby, and owners and the admin belong to every space and every aside. Someone in a space you don't belong to shows as "in another space", never with its name and never as in the call; someone who stepped into an aside from one of your spaces shows as "in an aside" or "in a private conversation", never with whom; you always see where you are yourself. A guest sees only their own space and its people. On home, a space card's "N here" and the switcher's "N here" no longer count people who stepped into an aside; their dot is off, and hovering over them reads "<name> is in an aside", "<name> is in a private conversation" or "<name> is in another space". **Who's around** uses the same words. The call's placeholder tiles read "In an aside", naming the others only for an ordinary aside (to its people, owners and the admin, and to those who saw it start without reloading since), or "In a private conversation", never naming anyone, also after a reload. When the stream is somewhere you can't see, the people you can place are marked "off stream", and those in another space or an aside you aren't in are not.
  - `GET /api/presence` is filtered by viewer (`server/presence-view.js`, `presenceViewer()` in `server/index.js`). Each user gains `aside`, `asidePrivate` and `elsewhere`. In an aside of the viewer's space that the viewer isn't in: `space` is the parent's id, `aside: true`, `inCall: false`, and `asidePrivate` says whether it is private, never with its members. Somewhere the viewer doesn't belong: `space: null`, `elsewhere: true`, `online: true`, `inCall: false`. `asides` lists only the asides the viewer belongs to. `activeSpace` is `null` when the stream is somewhere the viewer can't see, an aside they aren't in included. The access key (Coffee Pub Studio, OBS) still reads everything; `GET /api/status` is unchanged. See `architecture-overview.md`, "Spaces and calls".
  - For module authors: `host.presence` follows the same rules. Each person gains `aside` and `elsewhere`, `asides` lists only the viewer's, and `activeSpace` may be `null`. A keyed page, which carries the access key, still reads everything. No module changed.
  - The calls cap's refusal names the running call's space only to its members; anyone else reads "one is running in an aside" or "one is running in another space".
  - `aside-started` is `{ type, spaceId, members, private: false }` for an ordinary aside and `{ type, spaceId, private: true }`, with no members, for a private conversation. The space page reads a message without `private` as private.
  - Thomas has still to confirm one choice made while building: members of the parent space may see that an aside is private (`asidePrivate`), never who is in it, which keeps the placeholder that showed before. See TODO.
  - Checked by `tools/check-presence.mjs` (15 checks: the rules on their own, then a real server on a throwaway `DATA_DIR` against a stand-in LiveKit) and by new cases in `tools/check-nav.mjs` (`isHere`, `steppedOut`, `hereCount`, `asidePlaceholder`, `whereWords`, `offStream`). Verified live against the API and in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit. Not tested in a real call.
- The top bar's **Modules** menu is gone (Thomas, 2026-10-02, `plan-primary-nav.md` step 2b, decisions 30 and 31). It shipped with the new top bar (#147), but most module pages aren't places to go on their own. A module's page is reached from its card's heading on the spaces page, or at `/modules/<id>`; in a space, the space bar's **Modules** list shows and hides modules as before. Module notices still count on the bell, on the cards on the spaces page and on the space bar's **Modules** button. The phone menu no longer lists module pages. The top bar no longer asks `GET /api/modules/nav`, which stays for a module's own page (`public/module.js` names the page from it); `refreshModuleNav()` is gone from `brand.js`. Calendar (with To-do) and Map (with Places and Maps) are to become two direct destinations in the top bar, under a plan of their own. A module page without a card (Places, Stream) is now reached only by its address. No module changed. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (seven slots, no Modules slot in the bar or the phone menu).
- The top bar is new (`plan-primary-nav.md`, steps 1 and 2; Thomas's spec of 2026-10-01, approved 2026-10-02). It reads, left to right: the logo and the environment's name, both going home; **Modules**, a menu of every module page with its unread count (not shown when there are none); **Spaces**, where the word goes home and the arrow beside it (**Switch space**) opens a menu of your spaces, with "You are here" on yours, "N here" where people are, and **New space** for owners and the admin, which opens Manage's Spaces tab with its **Add space** button focused; then, while you are in a space, where you are ("› Disneyland", or "› Disneyland › Aside: Michelle" in an aside, where clicking Disneyland, titled "Rejoin call in Disneyland", does what **Rejoin call** does; picking Disneyland in the switcher from the aside does the same, bringing everyone back). Where you are always stays in the bar, cut short with "…": between 641 and 1000 pixels wide your picture shows without your name, and between 641 and 820 the environment's name is left out too. On the right are the bell and your picture with an arrow. Gone from the bar: the clock, the couch or suitcase icon beside the name (the logo box shows the uploaded logo, else the home icon), the light or dark switch, Manage, and the module page icons. The space bar no longer repeats the space's name.
  - **The bell** counts unread module notices for everyone, and for owners and the admin the module updates waiting; that count moved off your picture and Manage. Its list shows the notices newest first, with "N module updates available" first for owners and the admin (it opens Manage > Modules, over the space too), or "Nothing new."; opening it marks them read, and a notice arriving while it is open appears at the top, already read. Reading the bell on a page opened over a space updates the space page's counts too. A notice doesn't open its object yet, and there are no mentions (see known-issues).
  - **The menu under your picture**: View profile, **Dark mode**, **Manage** (owners and the admin), **Host console** (a host admin signed in to an environment on a hosted server), **Install as an app** (while the browser offers it, never for a guest) and **Sign out**. In a space, Manage and a module's page open over the call, and the host console in a new tab. A link to a section of Manage (such as `#modules`) now lands on it when opened over a space: the page's handoff query goes before the `#section`.
  - **Guests** see the logo, the environment's name and the space's name, nothing else (on a phone, no menu button), and always the environment's default light or dark look, whatever the browser remembered.
  - **On a phone** the bar is the logo, where you are, the bell and the menu button; the menu holds the environment's name, home and your spaces, New space, the module pages, then your picture and the entries above.
  - For developers: the pure parts are in the new `public/primary-nav.js` (`profileEntries()`, `hostConsoleUrl()`, `switcherEntries()`, `switchPick()`, `bellState()`, `anchorSegments()`, `crumbSegments()`); `brand.js` draws the bar and exports `setPresentSpace()`, which the space page calls; two new cancelable document events, `app:open-page` `{ href, kind }` and `app:enter-space` `{ id, action }`, let the space page open a page over the call or enter a space; a primary tool may say `phone: 'bar'` to stay in the bar on a phone (`phoneZones()`); `useDefaultMode()` and `toggleThemeMode()` in `theme-mode.js`. See `architecture-navigation.md`. No module changed. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (the slot order, the phone fold, the profile menu for each role on a single and a hosted install, the switcher, the bell, the breadcrumb, an old-contract module tool) and verified live in headless Chromium with a stand-in LiveKit at 1280, 1024 and 390 pixels, as a member, a moderator, an owner, the admin and a guest. Not verified: a real call, **Host console** on a hosted install (only its address, as a pure function), **Install as an app**, the breadcrumb in a real aside, and a screen reader.
- The home page has a word of its own, `home` (`plan-primary-nav.md`, step 1, decision 13), the thirteenth word key. Unless the owner or the template sets it, it is the space word's plural with a capital, so it reads **Spaces** as before, and **Trips** where a space is a trip, with nothing set. The top bar's **Spaces** uses it. Owners set it on Manage > **Template** > **Words**, in a **Home page** row with no article field; the host template editor has the same row. A refusal names it "The word for the home page…". It is in `/api/branding`'s, `/api/settings`'s and `host.locale()`'s `words`, and in the SDK's default words; `fill()` has no placeholder for it. Checked by `tools/check-templates.mjs` (its default following the template's and the owner's space word, a template's and an owner's own word winning, the refusals, `/api/branding` with a template) and `tools/check-names.mjs --words`.
- `GET /api/modules/nav` lists every enabled module with an environment page that the viewer may read at environment level, widget or not; `nav` is always `true`, and a manifest's `surfaces.page.nav` is no longer read. Maps and Research aren't listed, since they have no environment page. Checked by `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` (Places, Stream, To-do and Travel listed; Maps not; nothing signed out or for a guest).
- **For module authors:** `host.nav.set` ignores `bar` and `system`. A module's tools always go in the space bar; one written for the old contract (`bar: 'primary'`, `system: true`) is placed there like any other and is no longer refused. `surfaces.page.nav` in `module.json` is ignored: every environment page is listed in the top bar's **Modules** menu. Nothing else in the SDK changed; `host.locale().words` gains `home`. See `api-module-sdk.md`, "Registering into the nav bars".
- Guests get no install option at all (Thomas, 2026-10-01). On a guest link, and the windows popped out from it, there is no **Install as an app**, no install hint under **Join the call** in **Not in a call**, and no install line in the call's settings (the gear). The browser's own offer to install is dropped too. Before, a guest saw the hint telling them to use **Install as an app** in a profile menu they did not have. Members, moderators, owners and admins see the same as before, except that the space page now shows the hint, and registers its service worker, only once it knows who you are. For developers: `barInstall()` and `installBarred` in `public/brand.js` (run at load on a guest link's page; `loadMe()` also runs it if `/api/me` ever answers with the role `guest`, a guard for a future role, since no account can have that role today) keep no `beforeinstallprompt` event and remove the page's manifest link; `isGuestViewer()` and `offerInstall()` in `public/space.js` paint `#install-hint` and `#install-note` and register `/sw.js` after `/api/me` answers, never for a guest. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and reviewed by quality-assurance. Not tested in a real browser's install flow.
- Who is here, in the middle of the space bar, has a new look and says who in words. The portraits are small squares with rounded corners and a thin neutral border, side by side rather than overlapping, and "+N" is a square of the same size. There is no coloured ring on your own portrait any more: you are first in the row and "(you)" in the list; the only colour left is the camera mark on those on the call. Beside the portraits it says "Just you", "You and Alex", "You, Alex and Sam" or "You and 4 others" (first names, a long one cut short). When the bar is short of space it first says "5 in this space" (the environment's word for a space, or "aside" in one), then drops portraits, then shows an icon and "5 here", which is also what the phone's tab bar shows. Its tooltip still says how many are here and how many are on the call, and the list still reads "In this space (N)", with each person's name, "(you)" and the camera with "on the call". For developers: `hereLabel()` in `public/space-people.js` and the steps in its `fit()`. Read as code: `tools/check-nav.mjs` still holds `facesThatFit()`, `peopleIn()` and `hereWords()`, but not `hereLabel()` or the new steps, and no check of the new look in a browser was reported. Not checked in a real call.
- **Install as an app** moved from an icon in the header into the menu under your picture, between **View profile** and **Sign out**, and on a phone into the header's menu after **View profile**. It shows only while the browser offers to install. The hint under **Join the call** and in the call's settings now reads "For a window without browser bars, use Install as an app in your profile menu once your browser offers it." Someone without an account menu, a guest, no longer sees Install. For developers: the `install-link` tool is gone; the entry is in `accountMenuItems()` and, on a phone, the `account-install` tool in `public/brand.js`. Read as code only.
- The Conference switch always reads the Conference's name (or the name the template or the environment gives it), never "Rejoin call" (Thomas, 2026-09-30: "it is BAD UX to rename a switch"). Like every other switch, it only shows and hides the module. Switched on, the Conference shows **Not in a call**, and nothing asks for your microphone until you choose **Join the call**. Switched off (or closed with its x) while you are on the call, it hangs up first, so nobody is left on the call with the Conference hidden. On a phone the Conference tab's dot now means you are on the call, not only that the Conference is open. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit; not checked in a real call.
- Entering a space never joins the call, for members and guests alike (Thomas, 2026-09-30; this overrides decision 7 of `plan-entering.md`, which had guests enter with the call open). If the Conference is among what a space opens with, it opens in **Not in a call**. Joining is always a click on **Join the call** or the green phone, and the browser asks for the microphone only then. The guest form no longer mentions the microphone. For developers: the page connects out of the call (the token's `call` attribute is `off`, and the page says `off` again on connecting) and sets `on` only on a join. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and `tools/check-host-registry.mjs`, and verified live in headless Chromium with a faked LiveKit; not checked in a real call.
- Being pulled keeps you on the call (Thomas, 2026-09-30: "Pull keeps you on the call"). Being pulled into an aside or a private conversation, or back (**Pull participants back**, **Rejoin call**, or following the others back), keeps you on the call if you were on it, through the same join check and microphone rules as a click; if you were not, you arrive in **Not in a call**. Going somewhere yourself (entering another space, accepting an invitation, a module opened from the dashboard) never joins. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and read as code. Not verified with a real pull: an owner's pull into a real aside, and **Rejoin call** within the server's 15-second hold, need a LiveKit server.
- **Open with** on the space list, and **Opens with** and the **Modules** list in space settings, use switches in place of ticks, the same switches as the space bar's **Modules** list (`public/switch-list.js`). The words follow: "Nothing switched on: Chat and To-do open.", "Nothing switched on: nothing opens.", "None of the ones switched on can open now, so nothing opens.", and the space's modules list says "Switch one on to make it available to this space's members, in the Modules button above the canvas." Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and verified live in headless Chromium.
- The plan's calls cap counts only real calls (Thomas, 2026-09-30). Entering a space is never refused. A space counts as a running call only while someone there is on the call (their `call` attribute is `on`), not merely connected. The cap is asked just before joining (`POST /api/call/join`, see Added), and over the limit the join is refused with "This <environment>'s plan allows N call(s) at once; one is running in <space>", shown in **Not in a call**. Joining a call already running is never refused, and moving from one call to another never counts your own. A start that passes holds its space's place for 15 seconds, in memory for each environment, so two people starting calls in two spaces at the same moment cannot both pass. `POST /api/asides` asks the cap before pulling anyone, and answers 403 with the same sentence; an aside whose members leave nobody on their old call takes that call's place. `POST /api/token` and `POST /api/guest-join` no longer read `call` from the request, are never refused by the cap, and always start the person out of the call (`call: 'off'`); an OBS viewer's token carries no `call` attribute, as before. The environment's **Calls at once** and the host console's count use the same rule (the console's `usage.callsNow` on `GET /api/host/environments`, only for an environment whose plan caps calls; it was always 0 there). The cap is enforced by the page asking first: a modified page could skip the check, as before (see `TODO.md`). Documented in `architecture-environments.md` and `architecture-overview.md`. Checked by `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` against a stand-in LiveKit (entering over the cap, a second call refused, the running one joined, a guest, an OBS viewer, the console's count, two starts at once), and by curl. Not checked against a real LiveKit server.
- An aside's card on the space list reads **Join** with a phone icon ("for now", Thomas, 2026-09-30), and its pop-out button "Join in a pop-out window"; it read **Enter**, as plan-entering Part 1 built it. An aside is a call, so it never takes the word for entering a space. **Back to <aside>** is unchanged. Verified live in headless Chromium with presence faked (no LiveKit server here); **Back to** read as code only.
- The guest form reads as entering, not joining a call: the heading "Enter as a guest", then "Enter <space>" once the link is read, and the button **Enter** with a door icon (it was "Join" with a speech bubble). Both follow the environment's word for entering a space. The prose that said a person "joins" a space now says they "come in" or "come into" it, as fixed text: the guest link's hint in the call settings and in space settings ("Anyone with this link can come into this space with just a name…"), the Roles hint on Manage ("…everyone who comes in from a space's guest link"), and the product page ("People come in with a link… guests can come into a space with just a name…"). Verified live in headless Chromium.
- A space card's main button comes first, and the smaller buttons (the launch link, pop-out, choose what opens, space settings, module settings) follow it as one group, moving together to their own line on a narrow card. The main button's label reads in full unless it is wider than the whole card, when it ends with "…"; its tooltip always holds the full label. The **Open with** box opens above the buttons, so it never covers the main button. Verified live in headless Chromium at 1280, 1024 and 390 pixels wide with long words, with measurements.
- Your picture and name are the last thing in the header, at the right after the time, with a divider before them, and clicking them opens a menu with **View profile** and **Sign out**. The separate Sign out button is gone. The header's right end now reads: the light or dark switch, Manage and Install, then the time (with Call settings before it in a call), then you. A long name is cut short with "…"; a screen reader still hears the whole name. A guest has no picture and no menu, and the host console still hides this part of the header. **View profile** opens your profile over the space in a space, so the call keeps running, and brings a popped-out app back first; on your profile page it just closes the menu. **Sign out** forgets the light or dark choice made in this browser, as the button did. The ai-config and module-config pages now show your picture and name too. On a phone the header's menu holds Spaces, light or dark, Manage, Install and the time, each once; your picture first stayed in the bar there, and now folds into that menu (see the next entry). For developers: `#whoami-link` is now a button (ids `whoami-link`, `whoami-img` and `whoami` kept), and `#logout-link` is gone; see `architecture-navigation.md`. Verified live in headless Chromium at 1280, 700 and 390 pixels wide on the spaces page, /profile, /admin, module pages, module settings, space settings, ai-config, module-config, a guest's page, a hosted environment and the host console; Sign out checked (the session cookie gone, `/api/me` answering 401); a name with markup shows as text. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs`, `tools/check-syntax.mjs` and the other checks. Read as code only: the pop-out window and the space bar in a real call, since there is no LiveKit server here.
- On a phone your picture is no longer in the header; it folds into the header's menu (**Menu**, three lines) with everything else, as Thomas decided on 2026-09-30, reversing the keep-in-bar of the entry above. Open the menu: after a divider come your picture and name, only a label, then **View profile** and **Sign out**, which do what the account menu does. A guest has none of these. On a wider screen nothing changes. The phone width is the one where the header folds, 640 pixels and below. For developers: `keepOnPhone` is gone; the fold is the pure `phoneZones(byZone)` in `public/nav-bar.js`, and View profile and Sign out are the page tools `account-profile` and `account-sign-out`, shown only on a phone while your picture shows, sharing `viewProfile()` and `signOut()` with the account menu in `public/brand.js`; see `architecture-navigation.md`. Documented in `userguide-accounts.md` ("Your profile and signing out"). Verified live in headless Chromium at 390, 640, 641 and 1280 pixels wide on the spaces page, /profile, /admin, /modules/todo and a guest's page. Checked by `tools/check-nav.mjs` (17 tests). Not checked: a real screen reader (your picture read as a label through `aria-disabled`), the pop-out window, and a real call, since there is no LiveKit server here.
- **Leave space** in the space bar uses the same icon as **Sign out** (`right-from-bracket`). Checked in the page's markup; not seen drawn in a real call.
- The page's own menus work from the keyboard: the account menu, the canvas title bars' **…** and a module's **…** that the host draws. The first entry takes focus when a menu opens; the Up and Down arrows, Home and End move; Enter or Space picks; Escape closes it and puts focus back on its button; Tab closes it. Screen readers hear each entry as part of a menu, and the button says whether its menu is open. The shared menu moved from `public/module-host.js` (`toggleOverflow`, gone) to `public/host-menu.js` (`openHostMenu`, `closeHostMenu`, which module-host.js re-exports), and a page can take over **View profile** with the cancelable `app:open-profile` event; see `architecture-module-window.md`. Keyboard use verified live in headless Chromium. In a space with push to talk on, Space does not pick (see known-issues.md); Enter does.
- Theme, template and research files, and the block an AI writes objects in, are named by what they are, not by the product (plan-kind-names, the follow-up to #110), so the product's name can change without changing a format. A theme exports as `<name>.theme.json`, holding `{ "format": "theme", "formatVersion": 1, "name", "author"?, "light", "dark" }`. A template exports as `<name>.template.json`, holding `{ "format": "template", "formatVersion": 1, ...its fields }`, with its theme inside and unmarked. Research from another AI comes as one ```` ```objects ```` block or as `<something>.objects.json`, holding `{ "format": "objects", "formatVersion": 1, "objects": [...] }`; **Copy instructions for another AI** asks for those. `GET /api/objects/format` answers `fence: "objects"` and `fileSuffix: ".objects.json"`; its schema has no `$id`, and a file there requires `format`, `formatVersion` and `objects`. One rule reads the marker for all three (`server/file-format.js`). A ```` ```card ```` block is no longer the format's own: its objects are found only by the loose reading, which still reads an answer that lost its fences. The host template editor's theme picker no longer reads the file itself: the server does (`POST /api/host/themes/check`, under Added), and the picker shows " Left out: ..." after the theme's name when part of the file was dropped. The pages no longer write a product name of their own: they start with none and fill it in from the server's branding (the product page's title shows the brand, Coffee Pub, until then). Documented in `api-modules.md` ("The objects format"), `api-module-sdk.md`, `architecture-modules.md`, `architecture-overview.md`, `architecture-environments.md`, `design-theme.md` and the Themes, Templates, Chat and Assistant guides; the plan says built. Verified: checked by tools (`tools/check-themes.mjs`, `tools/check-templates.mjs`, `tools/check-template-switch.mjs`, `tools/check-object-format.mjs`, `tools/check-ai.mjs`, `tools/check-origin.mjs`, `tools/check-module-versions.mjs`, and the rest of `npm run check`, apart from the two `--migration` cases of `tools/check-names.mjs` that fail only as root in a cloud container), through two rounds of QA; verified live on a local server with curl and in headless Chromium. Not checked: the chat's **Bring in** button in a browser, which needs a call, and a real `/ai` reply, since there is no AI key here.
- **Files and answers in the old formats are refused, not read.** Anything named after a past product name, the one before Collaborator or Collaborator itself, gets a sentence saying what to do, and nothing is imported. A theme file: "That theme file is in an older format. Export the theme again and import the new file." A template file: "That template file is in an older format. Export the template again and import the new file." An objects file: "that file is in an older format: copy the instructions again and ask the AI for a new file". A pasted answer with a block fenced with a past label: 400 "that answer is in an older format: copy the instructions again and ask the AI for a new answer". An old-named block counts in any letter case, with words after the label, indented, inside a list or a quote, with Windows line ends, unclosed, and even beside an ```` ```objects ```` block, so half an answer is never brought in without a word; a label that only starts with a past name (```` ```collaborators ````) does not count. A real object with a key such as `relatedObjects` is still read as an object. "That isn't a <product> theme file." and "This theme was made by a newer version of <product>." are as before, as are the template ones. To move an old theme or template, export it again from this version; for research, copy the instructions again. Pasted into the Chat box, an old answer offers no **Bring in** button, because the objects check route refuses it (the route was checked; the button was not seen in a browser). Checked by `tools/check-object-format.mjs` (every case above, with the old files and answers kept in `tools/fixtures/format-old/`), `tools/check-themes.mjs` and `tools/check-templates.mjs`, and verified live with curl.
- `/ai` asks the AI for the part worth keeping in ```` ```objects ```` blocks and calls each one an object; it asked for ```` ```card ```` blocks. Its answers are read from blocks labelled `objects`, `summary`, `json` or nothing; `card` and the product-named label are no longer read. Assistant 0.1.22 (a comment only). Checked by `tools/check-ai.mjs` and `tools/check-object-format.mjs` (the rule's text); a real reply was not seen, since there is no AI key here.
- The version line's commit comes from `APP_REVISION`, which the image sets to the commit it was built from. `TAVERN_REVISION` is no longer read. Only the image ever set it, so nothing needs changing unless your own deployment sets `TAVERN_REVISION`: set `APP_REVISION` instead, or the version shows `dev`. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` and `userguide-getting-started.md`. Read as code in the Dockerfile and `server/index.js`; not checked in a built image (no Docker here).
- The server names itself when it fetches from elsewhere: `<product>/<version>`, from `PRODUCT_NAME` and the package version (for example `Collaborator/0.4.0`), for link previews, and the same followed by `(+https://github.com/Drowbe/coffee-pub-collaborator)` for place search, so the service can identify and reach whoever runs it, as the OpenStreetMap services ask. It sent `Collaborator` and `CoffeePubApp`. Only printable ASCII is kept from the name, spaces become `-`, and the default stands in when nothing is left. Documented in `userguide-places.md`. Checked by `tools/check-link-preview.mjs` and `tools/check-geocode.mjs`.
- `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on any product name written into the code: the default, read from its one definition in `server/product-name.js`, and the past names magpie and tavern, in any letter case, in any line (comments too) and in file and folder names under `server/`, `public/`, `modules/`, `tools/` and `templates/`, and in the `Dockerfile`. The brand, Coffee Pub, and the repository and image names are allowed; anything else only by a `product-name` entry in `tools/check-names-allow.json` with its reason (the read-once migrations, the checks that prove them, the default's definition, the two past block labels, the old fixtures). The checks whose sentences carry the name run with `PRODUCT_NAME=Testname`, so a hard-coded default fails them. If the default ever changes, the old one must be added to `PAST_PRODUCT_NAMES` by hand. Documented in `architecture-environments.md` ("The product name guard"). Checked by running `tools/check-names.mjs`, which also tests its own matching on made-up text.
- The space bar's **Grid size** goes one step smaller: 50 to 320px (it was 60), in steps of 10, 130 by default. A module still takes enough cells for its smallest size, and a saved size of 60 or more keeps working. Documented in `architecture-canvas.md`. Verified live in headless Chromium (the slider's smallest value is 50, the keyboard steps down to 50 and stops, and two modules snapped side by side with the grid already at 50 do not overlap) and checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` and `tools/check-nav.mjs`; not checked in a real call. Moving the slider can still make modules tiled side by side overlap; that was there before (see known-issues).
- Opening a space is entering it, not joining the call (GitHub #3). The space list's button is **Enter**, with a door, and **Back to <space>** while you are connected to that space; the pop-out button is "Enter in a pop-out window", and the sliders button's list is headed **Open with**. The card says "3 here · 2 in the call", "3 here" or "Nobody here" (it said "n/m Online"), and a camera mark shows who is in the call. The status line reads "in <space>" whether or not you are in the call. The space settings' launch-link hint says "next to Enter". Verified live in headless Chromium at 1280 and 390 px (presence faked; there is no LiveKit server here). Entering and **Back to** were read as code only. The guest form still says **Join**.
- A space you have not been in opens the chat and every module on in it, without the conference. It opened the conference. The order is: a fresh request from the dashboard, your remembered layout (never for a guest), the space's **Opens with**, the environment's list for a new space, then that default. A guest with nothing set gets the conference and the chat, and the guest form mentions the microphone only then (since 2026-09-30 the conference opens out of the call and the guest form has no microphone note; see Changed). A list with nothing that can open opens nothing. On a phone the first tab is the first thing opened, not always the call. Worked out by `whatOpens` in `public/opens-with.js`, checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`; entering itself, a first and a second visit, a guest's visit and the phone's first tab were read as code only, and a real call was not checked. Documented in `userguide-call.md`, `userguide-spaces.md`, `userguide-accounts.md` and `architecture-canvas.md`; `plan-canvas.md`'s old default is marked replaced.
- The conference switch holds for owners and the admin too. With the conference switched off, nobody is offered it in a space, and entering does not open it. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (`conferenceAllowed`); read as code only in the page.
- A command typed in chat always appears as a message, starting **private**. Its portrait is that command's icon and its name is the module's (**Polls**, **Calendar**, **AI**). **Make public** posts it later.
- A chat message is a header and a container. The header is a step darker than the message. On the left: a portrait, the sender's name, and the time. On the right: **private** or **public**, then **…** with **Copy**, **Reply**, **Send to...** and **Delete**. **Delete** on a chat message leaves "Message deleted". **Send to...** is a placeholder. Choosing private or public offers **Make public** or **Make private**. Making an AI answer public posts it to the space.
- Chat's formatting row is a short strip again. Formatting sits on the left; bring-in, commands and help sit on the right. Emoji, help and the command list open above their button and stay on screen. Bringing research in opens a row above that strip: paste a whole answer, then **Copy instructions**, **Choose a file**, **Close** and **Import**. Import puts the objects in the chat, and **Keep ticked** is where you decide what stays.
- The shared bottom row has one height, `--bar-h`. A second row in chat takes its space from the messages, so the other modules' bars stay `--bar-h`. **Private** / **Shared** sits on the same toolbar a module uses under its title, and only when `/ai` is available.
- The product is **Collaborator**. The GitHub repository is `coffee-pub-collaborator`, and new images are `ghcr.io/drowbe/coffee-pub-collaborator`. An existing NAS can keep its old data folder; point the compose volume at the folder you still have, or rename that folder to `/share/appdata/coffee-pub-collaborator` first.
- Chat's formatting menu icons: **Bring in research** is `square-share-nodes`, **Commands** is `square-poll-horizontal`, and **Formatting help** is `square-pen`.
- Chat's **Private** / **Shared** switch for `/ai` replies sits in the Chat header, next to **Clear your AI thread**, not in the formatting menu. It shows only when you may use `/ai`.
- Planner 0.7.44: today's day no longer has an accent bar on the left of the header. **· today** in the heading and the dot on the day chip still mark it.
- The prompt for Magpie's own AI and **Copy instructions for another AI** now asks for one fenced JSON array of every object, not a separate fence per object. An answer that still uses one fence per object is still read. Checked by a tool; not verified live.
- Research 0.2.20 and Planner 0.7.43: the in-page title is gone where the titlebar already names the module. Research's count sits next to the name in the titlebar. The Planner titlebar is the trip's Name; days and travellers sit after a | on the dates line.
- Chat /ai follow-ups from the docs pass: **Ask the assistant** asks in Chat (it no longer waits on a window that never opens); **Clear your AI thread** is in the chat header; Bring in research uses the same Assistant **Use** rule as `/ai`; a **Private** / **Shared** switch sits in the Chat header (Shared posts the reply to the space); new installs leave the Assistant out even if a template lists it. The Assistant's old window is gone (0.1.21). Checked by a tool (`npm run check`); not verified live in a browser or a real call. Documented in `userguide-chat.md`, `userguide-assistant.md` and `plan-one-input.md` (steps 7 and 8 built).
- The **...** on a card, a day, a place, a poll, and a module bar is one shared button (`host.ui.moreButton` / `.sdk-more`): the icon is centered. Research 0.2.19, Planner 0.7.42, Places 0.8.18, Polls 1.12.17.
- Polls 1.12.16 (#58 phase 2): the add box is **New poll**. Typing is `/v` in Chat, which opens the same form.
- A module's action bar puts the primary button on the far right and the rest to its left. As the window shrinks, leftover buttons come off the left into **...**.
- Research 0.2.18: **Add a note**, **Add a link** and **Add a photo** each show an icon and their words. A blank link form; a URL in Chat `/r` still opens one filled in. **Research this** in an object's menu asks in Chat (a private `/ai` about that object), not the Assistant window.
- To-do 1.11.14, Calendar 1.17.17 and Research 0.2.17 (#58 phase 2): each add box is a button (**New todo**, **Add event**, **Add a note**). Typing is `/t`, `/c` or `/r` in Chat, which opens the same form.
- Planner 0.7.41 (#58 phase 2): the add box is **Add plan**, with icon-only **Flight**, **Hotel**, **Restaurant** and **Note**. Typing is `/p` in Chat, which opens the same form.
- Chat's box now reads **Chat or type / for commands...**
- Chat (#58): **Bring in research** is in the formatting menu (file-import). **Copy instructions for another AI**, paste or a file, then Preview: a tick per object and **Keep ticked**. Pasting an answer still offers **Bring in N objects**, which now opens that preview. The command picker sits in the formatting menu, a **private** badge marks AI replies, and an answer's objects show as a preview with Keep on each. The Assistant stays a module (its **Use** permission still gates `/ai`) but has no window: `surfaces.canvas.menu: false` (0.1.20).
- Research 0.2.16, To-do 1.11.13, Planner 0.7.40, Calendar 1.17.16 and Assistant 0.1.19 for Chat commands.
- Planner (Travel) 0.7.39: when an object is added to the trip or its day is changed, the trip's first and last day grow to cover it — a date before the first day, a date or stay check-out after the last day, or the day a journey arrives. The range never shrinks. Import via the Assistant and Keep ticked use the same path.
- Assistant 0.1.17 and Planner (Travel) 0.7.38 for the research import: an object's text can be up to 6,000 characters, links may be `http` or `https` and are shown and kept, a waiting keep counts as sent, the action bus keeps a `text` field up to 8,000 characters, and the Planner stores notes up to 8,000. At most 50 objects per import; the Assistant's own replies stay at 20. Guests, and a space with AI turned off, cannot import; anyone else who may use the Assistant can, with or without an AI service.
- **Magpie is no longer marked MIT.** `package.json` now says `"license": "UNLICENSED"` (commercial, all rights reserved) and the README says so; the license terms, a server lock and purchased keys are coming (#72).
- **Breaking: `TAVERN_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `TAVERN_ADMIN_KEY` and `ADMIN_KEY` are no longer read, on single and hosted installs.** **Before updating, move the value to `ADMIN_PASSWORD`.** An install that set only one of them loses that password: a new install gets an admin with a random password, logged once; an install that already has accounts makes and changes nobody, and logs "This install has no server admin. Set ADMIN_LOGIN and ADMIN_PASSWORD, then restart, to have one." Each start that still sets one logs "<NAME> is no longer read: use ADMIN_PASSWORD instead."; the old "stops working in a later release" and "ignored on a server with environments" lines are gone. They join the names in `REMOVED_CONFIG_NAMES` (`server/index.js`). Documented in `architecture-environments.md`, `userguide-getting-started.md` and `plan-names.md`. `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` now covers each name alone on a new single install (the line is logged, the old value signs nobody in, a random password is made) and all three on a hosted server (one line each, the old value sets no password); its passing is QA's to report. Not verified live here.

### Removed
- The call control in the middle of the space bar ("2 in the call · Join", from `plan-entering.md` decisions 2 and 16, added earlier in this release) is gone (Thomas, 2026-09-30). Joining lives in the Conference: switch it on from **Modules** and choose **Join the call**. Its microphone note and install hint moved there. The middle of the bar now shows who is here. Checked by `tools/check-canvas.mjs`, which fails if the control comes back.

## [0.4.0] - 2026-09-25

### Fixed
- **Security: a page on another site could make changes as whoever was signed in.** A signed-in person's cookie still went along with a request from another origin on the same site (every environment is a subdomain of the host's domain), and a plain form needs no permission to send one. Now any POST, PUT, PATCH or DELETE that carries a cookie and comes from another origin is refused with 403 "This request came from another site, so it was refused." Not affected: Studio and anything else signing with a `Bearer` token, requests with no cookie, and `POST /login`, the landing page's sign-in. Follow-ups are GitHub #70. Documented in `architecture-overview.md`. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- Theme import follow-ups (GitHub #67): an environment holds at most 100 themes, and an import past that is refused with "This environment has 100 themes, the most it can hold. Delete one to import another."; control and text-direction characters are taken out of a theme's name and author, and a name is cut at 40 whole characters (never half an emoji); a file in an encoding the server can't read gets "That isn't a Magpie theme file."; a UTF-16 file imports; and Manage's Theme tab fits a phone. `userguide-themes.md` covers it. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- **Security: the stream access key could be used to sign in as anyone, an owner included.** `GET /api/status`, which the access key in every OBS link can read, sent every person's personal sign-in link, and a personal link signs its holder straight in. Separately, anyone signed in, a guest or a key holder could read every space's guest link (`GET /api/spaces`, `GET /api/presence`, the aside answers). Now a personal link is sent only to owners and to that person, and never in `/api/status`; a guest link only to owners and to members of that space who may manage its guest link (anyone else gets `null`). The environment's AI key and the host's managed AI keys are now encrypted at rest (a plain one is encrypted when first loaded), and `app.json`, `host.json`, `ai.json` and `secrets.key` are readable only by the server's own user, set on every start and after a restore or a migration. Moving a single server onto a hosted one keeps its AI key and two-step sign-in working, re-encrypted to the host's key. An AI key that can't be read on this server (data restored onto another host) no longer stops the other AI settings from saving; the AI settings say to enter the key again. **After upgrading, regenerate the stream access key (Manage > Environment > Access key) and give Coffee Pub Studio, or each OBS source, the new one.** File modes for the chat and module files, and some checks, are follow-ups (GitHub #69, in `known-issues.md`). Verified live on throwaway servers in every role, with the pages in a headless browser; the original exploit no longer works.
- Password boxes are masked (GitHub #61): Manage's new user, a profile's new password, and on the host console a host admin's and a first owner's. Each has a **Show** / **Hide** button, and is masked again after saving or cancelling. A check now fails on a password or secret field that isn't masked. Verified live.
- The Calendar no longer shows an error after the whole app is popped out, or when several modules close at once (GitHub #21). The chat in a window of its own now puts the cursor in its message box and sends on Enter; both used to show an error (GitHub #22). Polls and the To-do no longer show an error after they are closed, and the drop menu no longer fails when a drop offers two or more choices. Verified live.
- A link to a Calendar event opens the event again when the link is what first loads the Calendar (Calendar 1.17.11, GitHub #19), including the Calendar's space page and a Calendar just opened on a space's canvas. Before, it opened the Calendar but not the event.
- The page for a personal link that was turned off or replaced (`GET /j/:token`) shows the environment's name and icon (GitHub #18). Before, the page's own security policy blocked its script, so it always said "Coffee Pub". `tools/check-syntax.mjs` now fails on any page whose inline script its own security policy would block.
- Six things found and fixed while the pages were renamed (step 5b of the Names plan): inviting someone to a private conversation from **Who's around** always failed; Manage > Users never showed that someone was "in <space>"; a module's configuration never showed its line about each space's moderators choosing their own settings; the **Spaces** link during a call reloaded the page and dropped the call; the **Clear chat** overlay lost its styling to a broken style-sheet comment; and an old link to an item (`/modules/<id>?space=...#ref=...`) opens its item again. Verified live without a call; anything in a call is read as code only.
- On the Modules tab, a change the server refuses now changes nothing (GitHub #17). Before, a request that turned a module off together with the modules that need it, and also asked for something refused (an unknown run mode, say), still left them off while showing the error. Verified live, through the API and on the Modules tab.
- A Planner journey can be up to 7 days long (Travel 0.7.31, GitHub #14 and #15). Before, anything over 24 hours was cut to 24 hours, so a 24 h 30 min flight saved as 24 h and its arrival showed the same time as its departure. A day's time column now puts a later-day arrival on its own line ("→ 10:00 AM", then "the next day" or "+2 days"), and the cards and the editor say it in full. **Trip ends** sits on the day the last journey arrives, and after the plan's last day it shows the date over the time. A length over 7 days is refused with "A length can be at most 7 days (168 hours)." (a journey's length, a return leg's, and how long it takes from the stop before). The Bookings, Money and Decisions views follow the environment's clock ("9:40 AM" on the 12-hour clock). Verified live.
- A refused save now changes nothing (GitHub #16). Before, the parts of a save that were fine were kept even though the page showed an error: Manage's settings (a clock change sent with a currency the server doesn't know), editing a person (a refused role still saved the new login or name), a space (a refused link or profile still saved the name or description) and, on the host console, an environment (a refused status still saved the name or plan). Now the whole save is refused, and the error names the field to fix. A module update that is refused part-way can still apply its first part; that is GitHub #17, in `known-issues.md`. Verified live.
- Manage's **Currency** list shows every currency by its code and name, the common ones first, and the server refuses a code it doesn't know with "XYZ is not a currency this server knows. Choose one from the list, such as USD." (GitHub #4, the server's setting). Before, a request made directly could store any three letters. A code already stored is kept, and still shown in the list. The currencies are the ISO 4217 codes the server's own Node knows (`Intl.supportedValuesOf('currency')`, `CURRENCIES` in `server/store.js`). The Planner's trip currency is still typed; a shared picker is next. Verified live.
- The Planner (Travel 0.7.27) is clearer about journeys and stays (GitHub #5, #7, #8, #10, #11). A length is entered as hours and minutes and shown as "8 h 15 min", instead of a number of minutes. A journey's fields say what they are ("Departure day", "Departure time", "Flight time", "Departs from", "Arrives at", "Departure airport", "Departure gate", "Coach" for a train), and once it has a time and a length the form shows when it arrives, "the next day" when it runs past midnight (time zones are not taken into account); a flight's and a train's card say "Departs" and "Arrives". A stay has **Check in** and **Check-in time**, and **Check out** is now any date after check-in, even after the trip's last day, with its time; the stay's **Room** field is called Room again. The Bookings view shows a stay as a date range with its nights. The trip form asks for **Name**, **Where**, **First day** and **Last day**. Verified live in the Planner in a headless browser.
- Anthropic refused every request made with an organisation-level key ("This API key is not scoped to a workspace, so this request must include the anthropic-workspace-id header"; found the moment the AI diagnostics landed). The host console's Anthropic row and an environment's AI configuration page (custom source) gain a **Workspace id** field, sent as `workspace` and carried as that header by the server half; a key made inside a workspace needs nothing there. The seed from the server's environment is `AI_ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE`.
- The host console never got Font Awesome: `admin.<base>`'s own hostname resolver handed every request straight to `hostRouter`, whose catch-all 404'd `/fa/css/*` and `/fa/webfonts/*` before they ever reached the two static mounts that serve them (registered on `app`, since the bare base domain needs them too). Visible as the shared map files table's delete buttons drawing as empty red ovals -- any icon on the console was silently missing its font. Fixed the same way the bare base domain already exempts its own handful of paths: `/fa/` on `admin.<base>` now falls through past `hostRouter` to those mounts, Font Awesome Pro first if the admin has dropped one in, same as everywhere else.
- `server/region-cut.js`'s two "the world file address is not set up right" messages named a "Module Configuration" screen that does not exist anywhere in the product; reworded to "the world map address is not set" (`RegionCutJobs.estimate`/`start`, `validSource` false), leaving it to whichever page raised the error to say where to fix it -- on the console, the address field sits right above the region cutter itself.
- The same file's other two CLI-failure messages, in `reason()`, said "world file"; reworded to "world map" so every region-cut error shares the one noun -- "could not be reached" and, for a 404 or `NoSuchKey`, `answered "not found"; check the address` (that last clause replacing the same stale "Module Configuration" reference the `validSource` messages had).

### Added
- The host console's template editor shows module names instead of ids. `GET /api/host/settings` gains `modules: [{ id, name, icon }]` (the conference and the chat, then the bundled modules by id), and the console's template view of an environment always carries `source`, `version`, `appliedVersion` and `offerOpen`; for an environment that isn't open, `offerOpen` comes from the stored record, so it can over-report until the environment is opened. Documented in `architecture-tenants.md`. Checked by the tools only (`check-templates`, `check-template-switch`); not verified live.
- The host admin can make templates, and templates travel as files (plan-environment-templates, addendum 2, GitHub #68). The host console has a **Templates** tab: every template with where it comes from (**Bundled**, **Yours**, **Imported**), its version and how many environments use it, with **New template**, **Edit**, **Hidden**, **Delete**, **Import…** and **Export**. A template can now also give reactions, a theme and a set of icons, each applied once when an environment is made. Each template has a version: editing one makes a new version, its words, home icon and module names and icons reach every environment using it at once, and the rest is offered on Manage's **Template** tab ("Version N, updated: review what's new"), listing only what the template changed since the owner last applied or passed it over, with the reactions, the theme and the icons as unticked choices. A hidden template is no longer offered for new environments, and one in use can't be deleted ("Environments use this template: <names>. Hide it instead."). Templates export as `<name>.magpie-template.json` (at most 64 KB, its theme inside) and import keeping the file's version; a taken id asks for another. On a single server, Manage > Template has **Template files**: **Export**, **Import…** and **Delete** for an imported template not in use; on a hosted server an owner can only export the template their environment uses. A bundled template's version is raised by hand when what it applies once changes, recorded with `node tools/check-templates.mjs --update` and enforced by `check-templates` (`tools/template-versions.json`). New routes: `GET`, `POST`, `PATCH` and `DELETE /api/host/templates[/:id]`, `GET /api/host/templates/:id/export`, `POST /api/host/templates/import`, and for owners `POST /api/templates/import`, `GET /api/templates/:id/export` and `DELETE /api/templates/:id`; the offer and apply gain `reactions`, `theme` and `iconSet`. Not yet built: **Reactions** and **Icons** still sit on the Theme tab rather than moving to the Template tab. Documented in `architecture-tenants.md` (Templates, and the host API) and `userguide-templates.md`. Checked by the tools and a headless smoke test only (new cases in `check-templates`, `check-template-switch` and `check-themes`); not yet verified live in a browser, and full QA is to come.
- Owners and the host can switch an environment's template, or remove it (GitHub #59). On Manage's new **Template** tab, **Switch to** and **Switch**; on the host console, the **Template** choice on an environment's card. Words, icons and module names change at once, and the owner's own still win. Then the template offers what else it can add: its modules (ticked; one the plan leaves out is shown, not ticked, with why), and, unticked, its Lobby name and its new-space profile. Only what is ticked is applied (**Apply**; **Not now** keeps it for later behind **Review**). Nothing is turned off, and no data is touched. For developers: `PATCH /api/settings { template }`, `GET /api/environment/template`, `POST /api/environment/template/apply`, and on the console `PATCH /api/host/environments/:slug { template }`, `GET /api/host/environments/:slug/template` and `POST .../template/apply`; a switched template carries `switchedAt` and is never applied on its own at a restart; `templateHistory` in `app.json` keeps the last 20 switches. Documented in `architecture-tenants.md`, "Templates", and `userguide-templates.md`. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- Manage has a **Template** tab (plan-environment-templates, addendum 2, GitHub #68, step 2). It holds the template and its offer, **Words**, **Module names and icons** and **Home icon**, which has its own **Save**. Old `#words` and `#home-icon` links open it at that section. Each Modules card now reads "Shown as <name>" with a **Change** link to its row. Rows of tabs scroll sideways within themselves on a phone rather than pushing the page wider. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- Owners can share a theme as a file (GitHub #67). On Manage > Theme, **Export** saves the theme in the chooser, Strong Coffee included, as `<name>.magpie-theme.json`, and **Import…** adds a theme from such a file. An import is always a new theme (a taken name becomes "Name (2)"), is never applied until you press **Apply**, keeps its author's name, and carries only colours; anything else in the file is left out and listed. The API is `GET /api/themes/:id/export` and `POST /api/themes/import`; the owner's guide is the new `userguide-themes.md`, "Creating themes".
- A light or dark switch next to the gear at the top of every environment page (GitHub #62). It changes at once, without a refresh. A signed-in person's choice is saved on their account and follows them to other devices; a guest's stays in the browser; and once someone has used it, the owner's default no longer changes it for them. Manage > Theme's switch is now **Dark by default**, the environment's default. An owner's change of theme or default now reaches every open page without a refresh (a guest's within about a minute), which fixes pages needing a reload to show a new theme. For module authors: `host.on('theme', fn)` now fires on a change of mode as well as of theme. For developers: `PATCH /api/me` takes `{ themeMode: 'light' | 'dark' | null }`, users carry `themeMode`, `branding()` carries `themeMode` and `themeVersion`, `/theme.css` holds both modes under `data-theme-mode`, and the notifications stream sends `theme` and `mode`. Verified live in every role, in a headless browser.
- **Templates: an environment can be set up for one use when it is made** (step 3 of `documentation/plans/plan-environment-templates.md`, which is now done). The first is **Travel**: spaces are called trips, the Lobby is "Home base" ("Everyone on every trip."), the Planner shows as "Itinerary", the Itinerary, Places, Maps, Research and the Calendar are on in every trip with the chat and the call, the home icon is a rolling suitcase, and a new trip starts with the Participants profile. A template is picked only when the environment is made: a **Template** choice (and a **Plan** choice) on the host console's New environment form, the same on the product page's sign-up when the host has templates, or `TEMPLATE=<id>` on a single server's new data folder (ignored, with a log line, on existing data or on a server with environments; an unknown id stops the start). Its settings, modules, Lobby and new-space defaults are applied once; its words, home icon and module names and icons keep following it, and anything an owner changes wins, with **Reset** going back to the template's. A module it can't turn on (not in the plan, needing one left off, or Research until the AI service is on) is listed, with how to fix it, on the console card and in Manage's new **Template** panel. Backups carry the template's record. For developers: `templates/<id>.json` (checked at start and by `tools/check-templates.mjs`), `GET /api/host/templates`, `template` on `POST /api/host/environments` and `POST /api/product/signup`, and `template`, `ownHomeIcon`, `templateWords`, `templateHomeIcon` and `spaceDefaults` on `/api/settings`; see `architecture-tenants.md`, "Templates", and the new guide `userguide-templates.md`. Planner 0.7.35 rewords a sentence that read "A trip belongs to a trip". The chat and the call as switchable modules stay in `plan-optional-conference.md`. Verified live on hosted and single installs.
- An owner can show a module under another name and icon (step 2 of `documentation/plans/plan-environment-templates.md`). Every card on Manage > Modules, the built-in Conference and Chat included, has **Shown as**: an icon (the module's own first, then the environment's solid icons and every module's own) and a name (blank for the module's own), with **Save** ("Saved. Shown as <name>") and **Reset** ("Back to its own name and icon"). The card's title is the display name, with the module's own name, version and author beside it. That name and icon are shown everywhere the module is named: the header, the dashboard (beside a widget's own title, which stays), the Roles grid, a space's module menu and each module's header, Join with, pop-outs, Module Configuration, notifications, activity and messages, and the module's own headings. A refused name or icon is explained and nothing is saved. Uninstalling with the data deleted clears them; a plain uninstall keeps them. The text in a module's `module.json` (its description, widget title, permission and setting labels, help and options, event and action labels, kind names) now follows the environment's words too, through `{space}`-style placeholders, so the known issue about it is gone. For developers: `PATCH /api/modules/:id` takes `{ displayName, displayIcon }`; `GET /api/modules` adds `displayName`, `displayIcon`, `ownDisplayName` and `ownDisplayIcon`; `GET /api/modules/for-space` gives the built-ins, and notifications carry `moduleName` and `icon`; a module prints its own name from `host.info.module.name`, now the display name; `tools/check-names.mjs --words` reads manifests. Assistant 0.1.14, Calendar 1.17.10, Maps 0.7.16, Places 0.8.15, Polls 1.12.13, Research 0.2.13, Stream 0.1.6, To-do 1.11.10, Planner 0.7.34. Verified live.
- An environment can use its own words (step 1 of `documentation/plans/plan-environment-templates.md`). On Manage > Environment, **Words** lets an owner rename the ten changeable words (environment, space, aside, canvas, module, object, owner, moderator, member and guest): a singular, a plural and, when "a" or "an" is wrong, the word with its article. Blank means the default, **Save** saves them all, **Reset** puts one back at once, and a refused word is explained in the server's own sentence. The host's words (host and admin) stay as they are. Everything people read in the environment follows: the pages, the server's messages, the Roles grid, the built-in modules' texts, new spaces' names ("New trip", "Trip 3") and the bundled modules' own words; the text in a module's `module.json` does not yet (see `known-issues.md`). For developers: `PATCH /api/settings` takes `{ words }`, `branding()` carries `words` for all twelve keys and `GET/PATCH /api/settings` give the owner `ownWords`; modules get `host.locale().words`, `host.util.word(key, { many, cap, a })` and `host.util.fillWords(root)`; pages use `public/words.js` with `data-word`, `data-word-form` and `data-fill`; and `tools/check-names.mjs --words` fails on a changeable word typed into text people read. Assistant 0.1.13, Calendar 1.17.9, Maps 0.7.15, Places 0.8.14, Polls 1.12.12, Research 0.2.12, Stream 0.1.5, To-do 1.11.9, Planner 0.7.33. Verified live: with no words set, 44 page visits in every role and 28 API refusals read identically to the previous build; with words set ("trip", "hour" with "an hour", "game master", "player", "visitor"), everything checked read naturally; two hosted environments never shared words, the browser's cache included. Read as code only: the call page in a real call.
- A currency picker, shared (finishes GitHub #4). The Planner's trip **Currency** (Travel 0.7.30) is now a list with **Default (<the environment's currency>)** first, instead of three letters typed by hand; a trip saved earlier with a lower-case or odd code still shows it. Manage's **Currency** list is now exactly the list the server accepts, with names in the viewer's language. Under it: `GET /api/currencies` (signed in; 401 "sign in first") answers `{ currencies }`, the sorted codes the server takes, plus the stored code if it is not among them; modules get the same list as `host.locale().currencies`; and the SDK gains `host.ui.currencySelect(select, { value, empty, onChange })`, returning `{ set, value, destroy }`, which draws a "Common" group and then "All currencies" by name and keeps an odd stored value as its own option. Pages that are not modules get the same drawing from `window.hostCurrency` in `/sdk/host.js`. The contract is in `api-module-sdk.md`, "Shared tools". Verified live.
- Round trips in the Planner (Travel 0.7.29, GitHub #9). Any journey except a rental car has a **Round trip** switch; the return gets its own day, time, length, ends, number and seat, and an optional title. The booking reference, cost and payer are entered once and cover both legs, so Money counts the cost once and Bookings shows one row with both days and the route both ways ("LHR ⇄ JFK"). Each leg sits on its own day and names the other's ("Round trip · Return" and its day), a return dated before its outbound is flagged, and editing either leg opens both. Turning the switch off asks, and removes the return on Save; **Delete** asks whether to delete both legs or only this one, and deleting only the outbound moves the booking details to the return. A second return saved by mistake can be deleted from the form. Verified live.
- An empty day in the Planner (Travel 0.7.28) invites you to add to it (GitHub #6): it reads "Nothing planned yet. Click to add." ("Tap to add." on a touch screen), and clicking, tapping or pressing Enter opens the same menu as the day's "..." button, with the day already filled in when the form opens. Only people who can plan the trip see the invitation; others see "Nothing planned yet." A day with things on it is unchanged: its blank space does nothing, and you add through its "...", the + between days or the bottom bar. Verified live in a browser with a mouse, the keyboard and touch emulation.
- The Planner (Travel 0.7.27) has three more ways of getting there: **taxi**, **ride share** and **shuttle**, each with its own tile, card colour and fields (a taxi or ride share has a pick-up time, a travel time, and where it picks up and drops off). **Getting to this stop** also offers ride share. Verified live in a headless browser.
- The groundwork for renaming the code to the architecture's names (step 1 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). A server now refuses data a newer version of Magpie has updated, rather than misread it, and the host console can restore a backup. On a single-environment install, or when `host.json` is affected, the start stops with one log line naming the file. On a hosted server only that environment is refused: it answers 503 with a plain sentence, drops out of the product page's list, and its console card shows "won't open", why, and what to do, while the others keep running. Every card gains **Restore backup**: choose a zip, then confirm **Replace its data?**. A restore of a newer backup answers 400 and changes nothing; a restore whose data still won't open answers `{ ok: true, refused }`; a good one brings the environment back without a restart. Under it: `server/migrate-names.js`, the migration's frame with no parts yet (the record in `app.json`'s `version` and `migrations` and in `host.json`'s `migrations`, the copies in `pre-names/`, a staged commit); `refused` on each environment in `GET /api/host/tenants`; `server/studio-alias.js`, wired to bearer-token requests on `GET /api/me` and `GET /api/status` and adding nothing yet; and `tools/check-names.mjs` (in `npm run check`), which reports the old names left in the code by level, reports "room" and "table" in what people read (`--words`), and runs the frame twice over `tools/fixtures/names-v1/` (`--migration`), with its allow-list in `tools/check-names-allow.json`. No module changed. Details are in `architecture-tenants.md`, "The Names migration" and "Refused environments". Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`, single-environment and hosted, with the console driven in a headless browser; checked by `npm run check`. With no part built yet, the migration's writes and moves have been run only with stand-in parts in the check, never on real data. Nothing here touches the call.
- The agent team: `CLAUDE.md` (the project's instructions for Claude), seven agents in `.claude/agents/` (project-manager, product-planner, experience-design, server-development, bug-fixes, quality-assurance, content-manager) and the `/manage` and `/plan-feature` skills in `.claude/skills/`. `.claude/worktrees/`, which agent runs leave behind, is ignored.
- Two-step sign-in, the server half (`documentation/plans/plan-mfa.md`): TOTP (RFC 6238, six digits, thirty seconds, one step of drift, a code accepted once via `mfa.lastStep`), ten recovery codes hashed like passwords, and the secret encrypted at rest (AES-256-GCM, `DATA_DIR/secrets.key` on a single server, `host.json`'s `secretsKey` -- one key for the whole host -- with environments; neither ever rides along in an owner's export or the console's backup). New in `server/auth.js`: `totpSecret`/`totpCode`/`totpVerify`/`otpauthUrl`, `encryptSecret`/`decryptSecret`, `recoveryCodes`, and the pending token (`issuePending`/`readPending`, ten minutes, the `mfa_pending` cookie) alongside the thirty-day "remember this browser" one (`mfa_trust`, reusing `issueSession`/`readSession` directly -- same shape, same lifetime); `userStamp` folds in `mfa.version`, so a reset, a fresh enrolment, or a spent recovery code signs every session and trusted browser out at once. The user record gains `mfa: { secret, enrolledAt, recovery, version, lastStep, pending } | null`; `publicUser` exposes only `mfaEnrolled`. The pending step sits between the first factor and a session at all three doors (`POST /api/login`, the form `POST /login`, `GET /j/:token` -- a personal link is a first factor, not a bypass) and the host console's own (`POST /api/host/login`): `POST /api/login/verify` and `POST /api/host/login/verify` take a TOTP or a recovery code and finish signing in; `GET /login/verify` and `GET /login/enrol` serve the pages session's `login-step.html`. Enrolment (`POST /api/me/mfa/start`/`enable`/`disable`, and the host router's mirrored `/api/host/me/mfa/*`) works for the signed-in person or -- with no session yet -- whoever holds a valid `enrol` pending token; the QR comes from the `qrcode` package as an SVG string, never fetched from anywhere. An admin resets a member's factor (`DELETE /api/users/:key/mfa`, never their own); a host admin resets an owner's by login, never a key, since the console never learns an environment's own user keys (`DELETE /api/host/tenants/:slug/owners/:login/mfa`, a peer session's amendment to the contract). Policy is `settings.mfa` (`off | optional | owners | everyone`, default `optional`, through `PATCH /api/settings` and carried by `branding()`) for an environment, `HOST_MFA` (`optional | required`) for the console; a hosted environment's own host-admin cross sign-in is never subject to either, since its own factor is the console's. Regaining access: `MFA_RESET` (`login`, `slug:login`, or `host:login`, comma-separated, applied and logged every start rather than tracked as done -- the operator is meant to remove it again) and `MFA_OFF=1` (skips the step everywhere, logged as a warning on start, exposed as `mfaOff` on `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/host/me` and `branding()`). Found and fixed live, before committing: enabling or disabling a factor bumps or clears `mfa.version`, which changes the very session that just made the call's own stamp -- without reissuing it, the caller enrolling or turning it off would be silently signed out of the action they just took; all four routes (`/api/me/mfa/enable`/`disable`, the host router's mirrors) now reissue the session cookie as part of the same answer. `tools/check-auth.mjs` (new, in `npm run check`) covers the TOTP math (drift, replay), the encryption round trip and a tampered ciphertext, recovery codes, the pending token (scoping, expiry) and the stamp fold-in; `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` gained a group for the host admins' own mirrored state machine. Verified live end to end against self-hosted and `BASE_DOMAIN` test servers: enrolling, a real TOTP code closing the loop (computed with the server's own `totpCode`), the replay block, a spent recovery code refused a second time, the `optional` and `everyone` policies (including the no-session-yet `enrol` pending flow for a brand new account), the trusted-browser cookie skipping the step and an admin's reset killing it, the admin and host-admin reset routes (including the 404 for an unknown login), `MFA_OFF` overriding an `everyone` policy on an already-enrolled account, and `MFA_RESET` in all three forms.
- Modules register into the nav bars, and the bars are drawn from one registry (`public/nav-bar.js`, the "Modules register into the bars" step of `documentation/plans/plan-nav.md`). A tool is one registration, `{ id, bar, zone, icon, label, title, order, group, groupOrder, href or onClick, visible, toggleable, active, badge }`, and the host draws it in its own look; the primary nav's middle and right zones (Spaces, each module's page, your profile, Manage, Install, the clock, Sign out) and the secondary nav's right zone (Dock all, the stage-level snap and its slider, Full screen, Pop out, Pull participants back, Rejoin call, Leave) are now registrations of that shape, with every element id the code and the checks look up kept, and the phone behaviour (the primary's middle and right zones fold into the menu, the secondary is the bottom tab bar) unchanged. Groups draw with a divider between them and sort by `groupOrder`, tools by `order`, in the bands 1-10 core, 11-50 secondary, 51-100 utility, 101-998 a module's own, 999 last, so the system's tools stay ahead of a module's without coordinating numbers; `visible` is a boolean or a function, and a toggle's `active` and a tool's `badge` change in place (`setActive`, `setBadge`), never by registering again. The SDK gains `host.nav.set([...])`, `host.nav.setActive(id, on)` and `host.nav.setBadge(id, n)` and the `nav` event: a module's tools go into the secondary bar under its own namespace, drawn while its pane is open in the space and removed when the pane closes or the module is unmounted; the primary bar takes a module's tool only when it is marked `system: true`, the module's manifest has `surfaces.page.nav: true`, and it goes into the right zone, anything else refused with an error that says why. `/api/modules/:id/context` reports `module.nav` for that check. `tools/check-nav.mjs` (in `npm run check`) holds the ordering, band, visibility and namespace rules; the contract is "Registering into the nav bars" in `api-module-sdk.md` and the structure in `architecture-navigation.md`.
- The OBS pieces are a bundled module, **Stream** (0.1.0): the per-player view pages (`/view/<key>?s=...`, unchanged in URL, parameters and pictures, so Coffee Pub Studio and every existing browser source need nothing), the links page (every player's Player and Character links, and the access key with show, copy and regenerate, behind the module's `links` permission, admins always), and the box settings (borders, name plate, picture background and size, dim and tint for offline, aside and private) as the module's own server settings. It installs and turns itself on once per environment on the first start that carries it, taking the values the Server tab had, so no stream goes dark on the update. Built on generic host conduits, none named after it: a **keyed page** (`surfaces.keyed: { path, entry }`, a module page about one person opened with the server's access key and no session, served by `public/keyed.html` at `/<path>/<key>?s=`, one enabled module per path), `install: { auto, settingsFrom }` for a bundled module that arrives installed, the `color` setting type, and four SDK additions for a page that follows people: `host.presence` (who is where, the rooms, the active room, the reaction glyphs; `onChange` polls), `host.images.get(key, slot, { room })` (a person's picture as a blob URL), `host.media.watch(key, { video, audio, room }, handlers)` (a read-only viewer connection the host keeps, handing the module the media elements, the person's state and their reactions; page mode only) and `host.access.key()` / `regenerate()`. `GET /api/status` gains `pages`, the keyed paths currently served, asked for by Studio so it can say "install the Stream module" instead of showing a blank source. The host's own words for the stream key are now "access key" (the API names `streamKey`, `?s=` and `x-stream-key` are unchanged). Gone from the core with it: the Server tab's "Participant video defaults" panel (the module's settings form has every field, colour pickers included), the profile's "OBS link" row (the module's page lists every player's links), `public/view.html` and `view.js`, and `GET /view/:key` (the keyed route answers `/view` now). The plan and the contract are `documentation/plans/plan-stream-module.md`. Verified live: the module installs itself on a server that had the old settings and takes their values; `/view/<key>?s=` serves the module's box with those settings applied, refuses a missing key (403) and an unknown player (404); the links page and the settings form draw. The live camera path (`host.media.watch`) is a port of the old page's LiveKit code and was exercised only as far as the token and the connection attempt, since the development machine runs no LiveKit server.
- The server half of that contract: `modules.keyedFor`/`keyedPaths`/`keyedClaimant` (the last for the 404 sentence, checking installed modules and, in `index.js`, the bundled ones too); enabling a module fails naming the other when its keyed path is already claimed, and a keyed module can never be set to run sandboxed (media only draws into the page). `GET /<path>/<key>` is registered last, right before the error handler, so `GET /view/:key` keeps answering "view" itself for now -- the module's claim on it is real, just unreachable until phase 3 removes that route. `moduleViewer` gains a third viewer, a keyed one (the access key, no session): read-only, and only for a module with a keyed surface (`moduleAccess`), with `{}` permissions and a `{ key: 'viewer', name: 'Viewer', role: 'viewer' }` identity at `/api/modules/:id/context`. `GET /api/pages/:path` answers a keyed page's own module and entry.
