Verified live against the real Stream module (auto-install, the settings carried over exactly, the color setting's validation) and a throwaway one (the exclusivity and sandbox guards, a reserved path refused at install, the 403/404/200 route outcomes, a keyed viewer reading a keyed-surface module and refused a plain one or a write).
- Tenants, phase 1, the server half: one host serving several environments, each its own `store`, `modules` and the rest, from its own data directory (`DATA_DIR/tenants/<slug>/`), resolved from the request's hostname at the door. `DATA_DIR/host.json` is the registry (which environments exist, their plans, the host admins); `server/environment.js` builds one environment's full set of services; the names every route handler already reads (`store`, `modules`, `moduleData`...) are now Proxies resolving through an `AsyncLocalStorage` context the resolver middleware sets, so no handler body changed. With no `BASE_DOMAIN` there is exactly one environment and the app behaves exactly as before -- verified live, not just in the unit tests (see `documentation/plans/plan-tenants.md`'s progress note). With `BASE_DOMAIN` set: `admin.<base>` is a separate host console API (`/api/host/*`: login, list environments with usage, create one with its first owner, edit a plan, suspend, delete (moved to `tenants-deleted/`, never removed), backup and restore as a zip, host admins), the bare base domain a placeholder page, `<slug>.<base>` that environment, anything else a plain 404; `PREVIOUS_BASE_DOMAINS` 301-redirects an old base domain's hostnames to the new one; a pre-tenant install refuses to start until `MIGRATE_TENANT_SLUG` names what its data becomes. LiveKit room names are prefixed with the environment's own slug (the smallest safe thing ahead of phase 4's own call-service work). Not in phase 1: the owner role, enforcing plan caps, the console page itself (a parallel session's own work against this API). The `PREVIOUS_BASE_DOMAINS` redirect now carries the request's own port through to the new Location (found in the console's own integration testing): invisible behind a real proxy, where the port is implicit, but a redirect used to silently drop to the scheme's default port in local development. The console moved from `host.<base>` to `admin.<base>` before the first real use (the author's call: "admin" reads better to a customer) -- `host.<base>` is not kept as an alias, since nothing has ever depended on the old address; `host` stays a reserved slug alongside `admin` regardless.
- The bare base domain now serves the product's own landing page (`public/landing.html`, a parallel session's own work) instead of a placeholder, plus the handful of other paths it needs (`landing.css`, `landing.js`, `style.css`, `theme.css`, `img/site/icon`, `fa/*`) -- no environment is ever resolved there, so `theme.css` and the site icon both fall back to "nothing set" rather than needing one. `GET /api/product` (public, no session, answering at the bare base, `admin.<base>` and anywhere else it happens to be reached) is `{ name, contact, baseDomain, version }`; `name` (`PRODUCT_NAME`, default "Coffee Pub Tavern") and `contact` (`CONTACT_EMAIL`, default none) are configuration, never code, since the product's own name is not settled yet. `GET /api/host/settings` mirrors both for the console.
- What dropping one module's item on another does is now one shared decision, `tavern.refs.dropMenu`, instead of each module's own (an audit found four: Calendar and Planner built a menu of choices, copied between them; To-do and Polls only linked; Research always started a note; Maps looked one action up by name; Places and Assistant took no drops at all). A target says what is under the pointer (the drop context: the card, its own item there, the day, the time, the map position) and what it would offer of its own; the SDK adds what the dropped item's own module can do with it there -- set this task's due date to this day, link this task to this event, put this place at this spot on the map -- and shows one menu; one offer runs at once with nothing asked. A drop is about the item and what is here: a third module making something new of the item is not offered (the first version did, and a research note dropped on a plan day asked about a calendar event, a task and the assistant instead of just landing on the day). An action can say what the item it takes must have (`needs: ["place"]` on `actions.provides`), and the menu wears each offer's module icon, in the same look as `tavern.menu.show`. A drag can carry a card instead of a pointer, for a module with nothing stored: an answer the assistant wrote drags onto a plan, the calendar, a task or research as itself. Calendar's `createEvent` links the item it was made from; To-do's leftover browser-drag handlers are gone. Planner's typed-card placement (`acceptSuggestion`) is `fromSuggestion`, shared with its drop. All eight modules: Assistant 0.1.9, Calendar 1.17.3, Maps 0.7.5, Places 0.8.6, Polls 1.12.5, Research 0.2.4, To-do 1.11.3, Planner 0.7.17. The fill rules are `tools/check-drop.mjs`, part of `npm run check`; the design is `documentation/plans/plan-drop.md`, the contract "Dragging" in `api-module-sdk.md`. The chat as a drop target is left (host-side).
- The module window framework: a titlebar, an optional toolbar under it, the content, and an optional action bar -- the same four zones and the same overflow rule everywhere a module can be shown (docked, floating, its own window, its own page), documented in `documentation/architecture/architecture-module-window.md`. `tavern.toolbar.set([...])` is a new optional row under the titlebar for text, a tab switch, a progress bar, a slider or a button, reusing the same reuse-across-dock/float plumbing the frame and action bar already had, so a module's toolbar survives a dock<->float switch too; it is deliberately not a second row of titlebar icons, so a tab switch is text, not more icon buttons. `tavern.ui.viewSwitch({ id, options, value, onChange })` is the toolbar's first reusable tool -- a labelled view or filter switch that owns its own diffing and event wiring, so a module calls `.set(value, options?)` on every render instead of hand-rolling it. `header.set`, `bar.set` and `toolbar.set` now collapse anything past five items (or one marked `overflow: true`, always tucked away) into a host-drawn "..." instead of silently dropping it -- the host's own analogue of `tavern.menu.show`, for its own chrome rather than a module's frame.
- `tavern.menu.show({ id, items, at, anchor })`, a shared, reusable action menu drawn inside a module's own frame, replacing near-duplicate "..." menus each with its own positioning math and Escape/outside-click handling. Gained `iconColor` (an item's own icon in a fixed color, for a set of kinds already told apart by color elsewhere, e.g. Planner's marker types) and `href` (a real link, not a click handler faking navigation, so hovering, copying and opening in a new tab still work). Polls (1.12.4), Places (0.8.5), Research (0.2.3) and Planner's (0.7.15) gap-add menu are now on it; a module's own item menu with real form fields (a day picker, a type picker) stays native, since the shared menu only draws a flat list of actions.
- Polls (1.12.4), Calendar (1.17.2), To-do (1.11.2) and Planner (0.7.15)'s Open/Closed/All, Month/Week/Month+list/List, Open/Done/All and Days/Decisions/Bookings/Money switches moved off titlebar icons and onto the toolbar's `tavern.ui.viewSwitch`, each now text tabs instead of icons repeating the module's own name. Planner's two other titlebar actions (Hide/show empty days, Edit trip) are unaffected -- they are actions, not a view switch.
- A `tabs` toolbar item's options can now carry an icon, a label, or both (`iconOnly` keeps the icon and drops the visible label), the same shape `bar.set`/`header.set` items already had -- for a switch where the icon itself is meaningful, not decorative. Places (0.8.5) and Research (0.2.3)'s own Mine/This room/Everyone switch, previously a bespoke pill row built into each module's own page, moved onto `tavern.ui.viewSwitch` with it. `.mod-toolbar` scrolls horizontally instead of clipping when a row does not fit a narrow pane.

Verified live against a running server: the toolbar's tabs (text-only and icon+label), text, progress and slider render and dispatch events correctly (via a temporarily-instrumented Polls build, reverted after), Places' menu opens, arms and deletes a real place and its "Open in my maps app" is a real link, Planner's gap-add menu adds a marker with each type's own icon color intact, and Places' Mine/This room/Everyone switch (all three options, an icon and a label each) switches correctly down to a 280px-wide pane.

### Changed
- **Breaking: the old names are gone (version 0.4.0, step 10 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`; the Names plan is done).** Coffee Pub Studio must be v0.2.3 or later. The Studio alias is removed: a bearer-token `GET /api/me` or `GET /api/status` answers exactly as the pages get it, with no `tableName`, `serverName`, `rooms` or `activeRoom`, and roles `owner`, `member` and `admin`. **For whoever runs the server: these settings are no longer read**, and each start that still sets one logs "<OLD> is no longer read: use <NEW> instead.": `ADMIN_USER`, `TAVERN_ADMIN_USER` and `HOST_ADMIN_LOGIN` (use `ADMIN_LOGIN`), `HOST_ADMIN_PASSWORD` (use `ADMIN_PASSWORD`) and `MIGRATE_TENANT_SLUG` (use `MIGRATE_ENVIRONMENT_SLUG`). An install still using them: the admin signs in as `admin`; a hosted server with only `HOST_ADMIN_*` keeps its existing host admins but creates or resets none; the old slug moves nothing. `TAVERN_ADMIN_PASSWORD`, `TAVERN_ADMIN_KEY` and `ADMIN_KEY` are still read, with their "stops working in a later release" line. A new migration part, `names-copies-removed`, deletes each environment's `pre-names/` and the host's `pre-names-host/`; when other parts ran in the same start, it waits one start, so the copies survive the start that changed the data (a departure from the plan, the project manager's decision). The call-name fallback from step 3 is still read and goes later. Every level of `tools/check-names.mjs` fails now, and the allow-list holds no alias. Checked by a tool: `tools/check-names.mjs` (8 of 8 levels enforced, and `--migration`), `tools/check-host-registry.mjs` and `tools/check-auth.mjs` pass. Not verified live here, and not with Coffee Pub Studio itself.
- The documentation uses the product's names throughout (step 9 of the Names plan, GitHub #24): space, canvas, module, object and environment where it said room, stage, pane or panel, item or card, and tenant or server. Five wiki pages are renamed, so a saved link to an old page name no longer finds it: **Rooms** is now `userguide-spaces`, **The Call** `userguide-call` (was `userguide-table`), **Manage** `userguide-environment-settings` (was `userguide-server-settings`), the room layout architecture `architecture-canvas` (was `architecture-room-layout`) and the tenants architecture `architecture-environments` (was `architecture-tenants`); three plans are renamed the same way (`plan-environments`, `plan-linked-objects`, `plan-smart-summaries`), with git history kept. The Home page now also links the module window, navigation and environments architecture. Along the way, the documents were corrected where they described code as it was before the rename: the space bar's tools are registered with the nav registry (no `#snap-tools`), Leave is `#leave-space`, the canvas takes width with `takeWidthFromOthers()`, the dashboard opens an object with `openInSpace`, a widget runs in the `environment` scope with `host.spaces()` and `{ scope: 'spaces' }`, the drag's messages are `objects.ptrStart` and friends with `objectdrag` events, kinds are listed at `GET /api/objects/kinds`, a summary also carries `kindName` and `open`, and a module is switched on in the space bar, not from a Modules button in the toolbar. Documentation only; no code changed. Checked by `npm run check:docs` and `npm run docs:build` (no built page links to an old page name); the corrections were read against the code, not verified live.
- An aside is now the call and nothing else (step 8 of the Names plan, GitHub #24). In an aside the page no longer offers the chat or chat pictures, only the call; modules were already off there. The aside cards on the join screen look as they did. Under the hood an aside is no longer a space: `app.json` gains `asides: [{ id, members, origin, private, createdAt }]`, and spaces lose `ephemeral`, `origin` and `private`. A new migration part, `names-asides`, drops the old aside rows (asides are temporary; anyone in one during the upgrade lands back in a space) and keeps the original in `pre-names/names-asides/`. API: `GET /api/presence` gains `asides` (each with `mine`) and its `spaces` no longer include asides; `GET /api/status` gains `asides`; `GET /api/spaces` no longer lists asides; `POST /api/asides` and `POST /api/asides/invite` answer the aside's record; `POST /api/token` accepts an aside's id; an aside's id on any other space route (chat, settings, modules) answers 404 "no such space". Coffee Pub Studio, signed in with its bearer token, still finds asides in `/api/status` as old-shape rows in `rooms`, named with the environment's word for an aside, until step 10. No bundled module changes. Checked by tools (`npm run check`, including `tools/check-names.mjs`) and a short smoke on a local server; a real call in an aside is not verified, since there is no LiveKit server locally.
- **Reactions** and **Icons** are on Manage's **Template** tab now, after **Home icon** (plan-environment-templates, addendum 2, step 3b, GitHub #68), since a template can give both. The icon list, headed "Font Awesome" on the Theme tab, is headed **Icons**; nothing about either changed but where it sits. The Theme tab ends with a line pointing to them, and an old link to `#reactions` or `#icons` opens the Template tab at that section. On the host console's template editor, the rows under **Words** and **Module names and icons** line up: the name column is one width, so a long name or icon no longer pushes a row's fields out of line. Checked by the tool (`npm run check` passes) and a quick headless look at both pages; not verified live.
- The Lobby is for being together (GitHub #63): it has the chat, the call and only the modules made for it, which declare `surfaces.canvas.lobby: true` in their manifest (Calendar 1.17.15 does). Any other module is taken out of the Lobby when the server starts, and stays on in its other spaces with its data kept. Turning one on there is refused with a sentence naming the module and the Lobby; **Available in every space** reads "(not in <Lobby>)" beside it; the Lobby's settings page lists only the Lobby's modules; and a template's modules go in every space but the Lobby. Documented in `api-modules.md`, "The Lobby", `architecture-modules.md` and `userguide-modules.md`. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- The home icon is stored only when an owner picks one, so a template's home icon shows after a switch. A default home icon ("couch") stored by an older version is cleared once, on the first start. Checked by the tool: `npm run check` passes, including the new `check-template-switch` and `check-origin`, and `check-modules`, `check-templates` and `check-themes`; any live check is QA's to report.
- A thing a module holds is an **object**, and what is shown of it its **summary** (step 7 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). What people read says object where it meant item ("Link to other modules' objects", "...summarise and answer questions about the objects a person selects", "that object is no longer there"); the Assistant's results are "answers"; and a Planner entry linked elsewhere shows under the Planner's shown name ("Itinerary · Ferry"). **For module authors, this is a breaking change: `host.refs` is `host.objects`, with the same members, and `host.util.refKey` is `host.util.objectKey` (the old names throw, naming the new ones); `resolve`, `search` and the links answer summaries instead of cards; a drag carries `{ ref, summary }` with the type `application/x-host-object`, the drop context has `summary`, and `draggable`'s resolve returns `{ summary }`; `host.ai.ask({ objects })` answers `summaries` with `{{summary:N}}` markers (`items` is refused); and the manifest's `refs.produces[].card` is `summary` (the old field is refused at install).** A kind's name may use `{name}` for the module's shown name, and a new manifest field, `storage.renamed: [{ from, to }]`, has the server keep a module's stored keys matching the version running (renamed on start, install and update, moved back on a rollback, never over a key already taken). The routes are `/api/objects/resolve`, `/kinds`, `/search`, `/links` and `/api/modules/:id/objects/:kind/:objectId`, answering `summaries` or `summary`; `/api/refs/*` answers 404; the AI route takes `objects` and answers `summaries`. Each environment records the migration part `names-objects`, which changes no data. **For owners: the modules that ship with Magpie now update themselves on start to the version the server ships, whenever the update needs no new approval**; one that asks for more waits for you while the old version keeps running, and uploaded modules are never touched. Assistant 0.1.16, Calendar 1.17.14, Maps 0.7.18, Places 0.8.17, Polls 1.12.15, Research 0.2.15, To-do 1.11.12, Planner 0.7.37. Verified live on data upgraded from the previous build: links, backlinks, search, drag and drop, the dashboard, the Assistant with a stand-in AI service, Research's tags, and the Planner's plans before it is opened. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- The to-do list lives in GitHub issues (https://github.com/Drowbe/coffee-pub-tavern/issues): every open item from `documentation/TODO.md` became an issue (#24 to #58), and TODO.md is now a short index of them. The "a list is not an editor" rule moved to the architecture overview's new "Design rules" section.
- The canvas and its modules are called that everywhere (step 6 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). In a space, the two tools read "Dock every floating module beside the call" and "Snap every floating module to a grid" (in the environment's word for module); the Roles grid's group for the conference and the chat is **Modules** (it was Panes); installed modules' cards say "Space canvas" (it was Space panel); a space's settings say "Modules that open on a space's canvas."; and the product page no longer says "pane". **For module authors, this is a breaking change: the manifest's `surfaces.panel` and `panel.html` are now `surfaces.canvas` (`{ entry, width, height, mode }`) and `canvas.html`.** A module still using `surfaces.panel` is refused at install ("module.json uses the old surfaces.panel; use surfaces.canvas (Magpie renamed a module's panel to its place on the canvas).") or, if already installed, marked as needing an update; the bundled modules update themselves. `GET /api/modules/for-space` answers `canvas` instead of `panel`; the SDK is unchanged. For developers: `#stage` is `#canvas`, with the matching classes (`.canvas`, `.module-canvas`, `.module-floating...`, `.builtin-floating`, `.module-lifted`, `.subnav-modules`, `--canvas-cols` and the rest) and data attributes; `createCanvas` in `public/canvas.js` with `registerBuiltin` and `openBuiltin`; `tools/check-canvas.mjs` (was `check-room-layout.mjs`); and `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on stage, pane and panel for these levels. Assistant 0.1.15, Calendar 1.17.12, Maps 0.7.17, Places 0.8.16, Polls 1.12.14, Research 0.2.14, To-do 1.11.11, Planner 0.7.36. Verified live without a call: an upgrade from the previous build keeps everything, saved canvas layouts included; docking, floating, snapping, resizing and pop-outs; the phone's tab bar. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- Modules speak space and environment too (step 5c of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). **For module authors, this breaks every module written for an earlier Magpie.** The SDK and `module.json` take only the new names, and an old one is refused with a sentence naming the new word: `info.context` is `{ scope: 'environment' | 'space' | 'keyed', spaceId }`; storage scopes are `environment`, `space`, `spaces` and `person`, and `change` events carry `spaceId`; `host.rooms()` is `host.spaces()`; a pointer is `{ module, kind, id, scope: 'environment' | 'space' | 'person', space }`; `images.get` and `media.watch` take `{ space }`, `follow(spaceId)`; `host.presence` answers `{ people: [{ key, name, online, space, inCall, isOwner }], spaces, asides, activeSpace, ownerOnline, reactions }`; `notify` and `schedule` take `to` as `space`, `environment` or a person's key. A manifest's `scope`, a setting's `scope` and `install.settingsFrom` use `environment`, `space` and `person`, and permission defaults are keyed `member`, `moderator` and `guest`; anything else is refused at upload with 400. A permission can name the key it had before with `replaces`, and each role's choice carries over. `host.refs` and the resolved "card" keep their names until step 7. An installed module in the old names stays installed but can't run: the Modules tab marks it **Needs an update**, with the reason, and offers no Enable; its old versions are marked "(needs an update)"; turning it on, or rolling back to such a version, answers 409, and a module that requires it doesn't run either. A module runs only while everything it requires is running, at any depth, and comes back by itself when that does; a module that can't run shows no Module Configuration link and isn't counted under Configurable, and the card's header wraps at phone width. **For owners: the nine modules that ship with Magpie update themselves on the first start, keeping on or off, their spaces and their data** (Assistant 0.1.12, Calendar 1.17.8, Maps 0.7.14, Places 0.8.13, Polls 1.12.11, Research 0.2.11, Stream 0.1.4, To-do 1.11.8, Planner 0.7.32); a new permission that is off for every role needs no approval on that path, and anything else they ask for waits for you; they update the ones others need first, and one that can't be turned back on stays off, the log saying why. Links between modules saved before keep working: each environment's first start rewrites the old pointers in modules' data and in stored events (recorded; the originals in `pre-names/names-pointers/`). Stream has a new permission, **See the Stream page**, off for members, moderators and guests (a role that had **See the stream links and the access key** keeps it), and the access key stays with owners and the admin. Places' setting is now **Save places in this environment**, and Places' and Research's saved views go back to their default once. Presence and `/api/status` say `isOwner` and `ownerOnline`, and the aside pull topic `byOwner`. On a hosted server, each environment's startup lines start with `[<slug>] `. Verified live on single and hosted data upgraded from the previous build: all nine modules, the links between them, the dashboard, drag and drop, the Stream page for each role, and a module in the old names refused. Read as code only: anything in a call.
- The pages say space and environment too (step 5b of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). Manage's first tab is **Environment** (it was Server), opening with **Name and icon** and its **Name** field, and the rooms tab is **Spaces**; the profile's tab is **Spaces**; the crumb on a space's settings, a profile being edited, a module's configuration and the AI configuration says **Manage** (it said Server Settings); a new space is called "Space N", the Spaces tab counts "N spaces", and a built-in module's card says "On a space's canvas". Saved links keep working: `/admin#server`, `#settings` and `#rooms`, the profile's `#rooms`, and `/room.html`, `/roomconfig.html` and `/module-settings?room=` (now `?space=`, answered with a 301 keeping the rest of the query). Each browser keeps its layout: the saved canvas (`app.panels`, `app.panels.<id>`, `tavern.panels.<id>`) moves to `app.canvas` once on load, and the current space (`host.room`) to `app.space`, each only where the new key is empty; chat keys keep their names. For developers: `room.html`/`room.js` are `space.html`/`space.js`, `roomconfig.*` is `space-settings.*` and `room-modules.js` is `canvas.js`; ids and classes use space names; `mountModule` in `public/module-host.js` takes `environment` or `space` and a `spaceId`, while modules still see the old SDK names until step 5c; `tools/check-module-host.mjs` is new, and `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on "room" in `public/` too. Verified live on installs upgraded from the previous build, fresh and hosted, as an owner, a member, a guest and a keyed viewer: every page, the old hashes and addresses, old browser keys including malformed ones, a phone width and the keyboard. Read as code only: anything in a call.
- The server and its stored data say space and environment (step 5a of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). **On the first start after this update, every environment's stored data is renamed: `rooms` becomes `spaces` in `app.json` and `chat.json`, `serverName` becomes `environmentName`, the image folders `rooms/` become `spaces/`, and each module's `server.json` and `room-<id>.json` become `environment.json` and `space-<id>.json`, with its uploads, links, events, schedules and notifications to match. It is recorded in `app.json`, and the originals are kept in `pre-names/names-spaces/`. Links people saved keep working: `/rooms/<id>`, `/img/room/<id>`, picture addresses with `room=` and `roomOnly=1`, and a module popped out with `moduleRoom=` redirect for good to their new addresses. Reload any call page that was open during the update.** The routes are `/api/spaces...`, `/api/me/spaces/:spaceId...`, `/api/users/:key/spaces/:spaceId...`, `/api/modules/for-space?space=` and `/api/modules/:id/spaces-data`; the old `/api/rooms...` paths answer 404. Fields follow: presence and `/api/status` give `spaces`, `activeSpace` and each person's `space`; `environmentName` replaces `serverName`; `POST /api/token` takes `space` and answers `call` and `spaceId`; an aside answers `{ aside }` and a return `{ space }`, and the aside topics carry `spaceId` and `spaceName`. Module routes take `scope=environment`, `space`, `spaces` or `person` with `space=` (no scope means environment), and an old scope is refused with a sentence saying which are allowed. The server's sentences say "space", and the Roles grid's group is "In the Space". Coffee Pub Studio, signing in with its token, still gets `serverName`, `rooms`, `activeRoom` and `users[].online.room`. Modules see no change yet: the SDK and manifests keep `server`, `room` and `roomId` until step 5c, and the host translates; no bundled module changed. `tools/check-names.mjs` fails on "room" in `server/` and `tools/`. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: single and hosted data made by the previous build, and a backup made before the update restored after it, each read back identically, with 20 access checks unchanged, the redirects, Studio's token answers against the pages', the pages as an owner, a member and a guest, and all nine modules' pages. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- The compose file holds only the server's admin, never an owner (a follow-up to step 4 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`; Thomas: "The compose file should be for the admin of the entire server... they own environments."). `ADMIN_LOGIN` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD` make the server's admin on every install and reset its password on each start when it differs. On a hosted server that is the host admin (`HOST_ADMIN_LOGIN` and `HOST_ADMIN_PASSWORD` still work, with a line in the log; if the two passwords differ, `ADMIN_PASSWORD` wins, with a warning). On a single install it is the environment's **admin**: every right, not an owner, shown as "Admin: runs this server", and its role, login, password, personal link and two-step sign-in can't be changed or reset from Manage, nor can it be deleted. Owners are made in Manage, and an environment may have none ("keep at least one owner" is gone). The lockout bypass covers the admin. **`OWNER_PASSWORD`, added in step 4, is gone: it is ignored, with a line in the log. If you set it, put the same login and password in `ADMIN_LOGIN` and `ADMIN_PASSWORD` before you update, and that account becomes the admin again on the next start; without them, the owners run the server and there is no admin.** With no `ADMIN_PASSWORD`, a brand-new install makes an admin with a random password, logged once, and an install with accounts makes nobody and says so in the log. `ADMIN_USER` and `TAVERN_ADMIN_USER` are now read as `ADMIN_LOGIN`, and `TAVERN_ADMIN_PASSWORD` and `TAVERN_ADMIN_KEY` as `ADMIN_PASSWORD`, each with a line. Moving a single install onto a hosted server makes its admin the new environment's owner. `docker-compose.yml` has one `ADMIN_LOGIN` / `ADMIN_PASSWORD` pair. `PORT=0` now works and logs the real port. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: a fresh single install, one upgraded by step 4, and a hosted install with each combination of the variables, with the pages in a browser. Checked by `npm run check`.
- The person who runs an environment is an **owner** and everyone else a **member**, on every install (step 4 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). The words were Admin and User on a single server and Owner only on a hosted one; now the Roles grid's columns are Owner (locked, "Owners can always do this"), Moderator, Member and Guest everywhere, and a profile says "Owner: runs the environment", "Member" or, for the host admin's own account inside an environment, "Host admin: runs this server". Each environment's first start renames the stored roles (`admin` to `owner`, `user` to `member`, in the accounts, `settings.roles` and invites), records that in `app.json`, and keeps the original in `pre-names/names-roles/`. **On a hosted server the host admin's account in each environment becomes `admin` with every right, even where an owner had changed its role before.** That account's role can't be changed, and it doesn't count toward "keep at least one owner". A single install's configured account is an owner: set `OWNER_PASSWORD`; `ADMIN_PASSWORD` still works, logging on each start that the name has changed, and `OWNER_PASSWORD` wins if both are set (`ADMIN_USER`, `ADMIN_KEY` and `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS` keep their names). The roles route is `PATCH /api/roles/member` (`/api/roles/user` answers 404), and owner-only requests answer 403 "owners only". Coffee Pub Studio, signing in with its token, still sees the old values (`admin` for an owner or the host admin, `user` for a member). Modules ask `host.can()` instead of reading the role, and an owner has every module permission; a permission key must match `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]{0,23}$`. Polls 1.12.10 adds **Close and delete polls other people started** and Research 0.2.10 **Remove photos other people added**, both off by default, so each update waits for an owner's approval once. Maps 0.7.13 with no map files tells everyone "Maps is not set up yet. An owner adds map files in Manage. Your places are in the Places module."; Stream 0.1.3 shows the access key panel to owners and the host admin; Assistant 0.1.11 says owner. A restore now refuses a backup whose `app.json` can't be read, before replacing anything: 400 "This backup's data can't be read, so nothing was restored." `tools/check-names.mjs` now fails on the old role values in code and in words. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: single and hosted data made by the previous build, with every right compared before and after the upgrade item by item, and the pages and modules in a headless browser. Checked by `npm run check`. Read as code only: anything in a real call.
- The call is the call, not "the table" (step 3 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). Every page now says "the call" or "a space" where it said "the table": the call page, Manage ("N of M in a call"), the profile, the invitation ("You're invited to join <environment name>."), a space's settings and the product page. Each environment's first start removes the two settings the call was named from (`tableName` and `settings.room`), records that in `app.json` (now `version: 2`), and keeps the original in `pre-names/names-table/`. The Lobby's description changes to "Where everyone meets." only if it was still the old default. Calls are now named from the space: `lobby`, `<id>` and `aside-<id>`, each prefixed `<slug>.` on a hosted server, and nothing about the name is stored. For one release the old names are still recognised, so people already in a call during the update are found, placed, muted and kicked where they are. **After updating, reload any call page that was open**: until it is reloaded it does not follow a pull aside, a recall or a return, or show who is online. The routes are `GET /api/presence` (was `GET /api/table`) and `POST /api/asides`, `/api/asides/invite`, `/api/asides/invite/:id/decline`, `/api/asides/recall` and `/api/asides/return` (was `/api/table/...`, which now answers 404); the data-channel topics are `aside-pull`, `aside-started`, `aside-recall` and `aside-return`. `room` and `tableName` are gone from the branding every page reads (`/api/branding`, `/api/config`, `/api/me`, `/api/settings`, `/api/presence`), and `table` from `/api/status`; Coffee Pub Studio, signing in with its token, still gets `tableName`, set to the environment's name. A browser's saved call preferences move from `host.table` to `app.call` on load. Stream 0.1.2 says "in the call". `tools/check-names.mjs` now fails on "table" in code and in words. Fixed along the way: another environment's calls no longer count against an environment's calls cap. An environment whose `app.json` is there but is not valid JSON, or is not an object, is now refused instead of opening empty and being written over on its first save: a single install stops with the file named, and a hosted environment answers 503 ("This environment's data could not be read. The host admin has been told.") and shows "won't open" on the console, so restoring a broken backup no longer leaves an empty environment. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: single-environment and hosted data made by the previous build, a stand-in LiveKit answering the server's room-service calls (for the call names, the calls cap, placement, mute and kick), and the pages in a headless browser without a call. Read as code only: a real call, meaning the media, tokens joining the new names at LiveKit, moving between calls, and the data topics in a browser.
- A hosted server's environments are called environments on disk and in the host API (step 2 of `documentation/plans/plan-names.md`). On its first start, a server with environments renames `DATA_DIR/tenants/` to `environments/`, `tenants-deleted/` to `environments-deleted/` and `host.json`'s `tenants` to `environments`, records that in `host.json`'s `migrations`, and keeps the original `host.json` in `pre-names-host/names-environment/`. A `host.json` listing different environments under both keys stops the start with a sentence saying which file to fix; nothing is changed. Moving a single-environment install onto a hosted server now takes `MIGRATE_ENVIRONMENT_SLUG`; `MIGRATE_TENANT_SLUG` still works, with a log line on each start saying to change it. The host API is `/api/host/environments...` (list, create, edit, delete, backup, restore, an owner's second-factor reset); the old `/api/host/tenants...` paths answer 404, and `POST /api/host/billing` answers `{ environment }` instead of `{ tenant }`. The migration now keeps a note of each folder it moves, so a start stopped between two moves still records every move when it runs again, and its error messages say where a folder or the original files are. On the console: a suspended environment's button reads **Resume** (it said **Restore**, beside **Restore backup**); focus returns to **Restore backup** after a restore, and to the message line after a delete; a card that won't open no longer offers the owner's second-factor reset, which could not work; and **Save plans**, the new environment's slug and the product page's sign-up slug are checked by the browser before sending, with a blank plan name now refused. `tools/check-names.mjs` now fails on `tenant` in code and in words. Verified live on throwaway servers under `/tmp`: a hosted data folder made by the previous build, with active, suspended, deleted and refused environments, upgraded and opened, and a backup made before the upgrade restored after it; a single-environment install behaving as before; the console driven by keyboard in a headless browser. Checked by `npm run check`, with interrupted starts simulated by killing the process (`SIGKILL`), not by a power loss. No module changed, and nothing here touches the call.
- Finished work is committed once it has passed QA and has its documentation, so Thomas can pull the image and test it: `CLAUDE.md`'s rule no longer waits for him to commit, the project manager makes the commit, and the other agents still don't commit. Pushing and publishing the wiki still wait for him to ask.
- The naming rule: `CLAUDE.md` has a Names section (host, environment, space, aside, canvas, module, object; admin, owner, moderator, member, guest), used in code and in words, and the rule that "the code keeps `room`" is gone. The agent files in `.claude/agents/` follow it. The code still says room and tenant until the Names plan's later steps rename it.
- The project-manager agent now plans in two halves, because an agent it calls can't talk to Thomas. product-planner researches the options and the open questions. The project manager puts them to Thomas, a few at a time. product-planner then writes the plan from his decisions. Nothing is built until Thomas approves a short summary of the plan.
- Self-service sign-up on the product page is off unless the server opts in with `SIGNUP: "on"` (the author, on finding the form live on their host: "Not ready for this"). It was on by default with `SIGNUP=off` as the way out; a freshly upgraded host now offers no sign-up form until the operator turns it on. With it off, every call to action on the product page (the nav's Get started, the hero's Get your own environment, every plan's button) reads **Coming soon** and does nothing, and the Get started band is titled Coming soon with a mail link to ask only when the host has given a contact address. The compose sample and the getting-started guide say so; the server half is its own entry.
- The server half of the above: `signupEnabled` flips from `SIGNUP !== 'off'` to `SIGNUP === 'on'`, so `POST /api/product/signup` and `GET /api/product`'s own `signup` field both default closed with no `SIGNUP` variable set at all, same as a host that has never heard of it. Verified live: no `SIGNUP` set answers `signup: false` and the sign-up route its plain "sign-up is off"; `SIGNUP=on` still opens it, unchanged.
- Two-step sign-in's switches, the pages half, after the author's redesign ("durable settings with booleans so the admin need not guess"): three layered switches instead of a four-way policy and two set-once-then-remove variables. The server offers it or not (`ENABLE_MFA`): when not, the profile's Two-step row, Manage's switch and the console's Host-tab panel are not shown at all (`mfaOffered` on `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/host/me` and `branding()`). Each environment requires it or not: Manage > Server's select became one checkbox, **Require two-step sign-in for everyone** (`settings.mfaRequired`), and the sign-in page's note keys off `branding().mfaRequired`. Each person chooses on their profile when it is offered and not required. Getting back in is `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS`, a plain boolean: while it is on, an admin's profile and a host admin's Host tab say "on, bypassed", hide Turn off and offer **Reset my second factor** confirmed with the password (`POST /api/me/mfa/reset` and `/api/host/me/mfa/reset`; `mountDisable` in `public/mfa-enrol.js` gained a `password: true` variant), and Manage and the console carry a banner saying to turn it off again (`mfaBypass`). `MFA_OFF`, `MFA_RESET`, `HOST_MFA=required` and their banners are gone from the pages and the guides (`HOST_MFA_REQUIRED` is the console's requirement now); the compose sample carries the two booleans set to their defaults, uncommented, so they are seen. The plan's "Regaining access" is rewritten as "The server's two switches".
- Two-step sign-in's switches, the server half, matching the pages entry above. `ENABLE_MFA` (default `true`) replaces the whole feature's on/off: when `false`, no door ever issues a pending step regardless of an environment's own policy, and every enrolment and reset route (`/api/me/mfa/*`, `/api/host/me/mfa/*`) answers 403 `{ error: 'two-step sign-in is not offered on this server' }` -- nothing already enrolled is touched or deleted, it just cannot be reached while the switch is off. `settings.mfa`'s four-way string becomes `settings.mfaRequired`, a plain boolean through `PATCH /api/settings`; a saved `'everyone'` migrates to `true` on load (anything else to `false`, since `'owners'`-only had no boolean equivalent and `'off'` is now the server's own `ENABLE_MFA`), the old key dropped from what `updateSettings` builds so the very next save leaves it out of the file for good (`server/store.js`'s new `migrateMfaSettings`). `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS` (default `false`) replaces the old `MFA_RESET`/`MFA_OFF` pair with one durable switch: while it is on, every admin (an environment's own, and a host admin) skips the pending step and the enrol requirement entirely -- members are still asked -- and a new `POST /api/me/mfa/reset { password }` (mirrored `/api/host/me/mfa/reset`) clears just the caller's own factor by password rather than a code, for an admin who has one enrolled but cannot produce it; refused with 403 for anyone it does not apply to, and logged as a warning on every start while it is on, the same as an open registration or a missing secret would be. `HOST_MFA_REQUIRED` (`true`/`false`) replaces `HOST_MFA=required` for the console; the old spelling is still honoured. `GET /api/me`, `GET /api/host/me` and `branding()` all carry `mfaOffered` and (on the two `/me` routes) `mfaBypass`, `mfaOffered`'s and `mfaBypass`'s session-specific truth for that exact caller. Not released to any real install yet, so `MFA_RESET`/`MFA_OFF` needed no compatibility shim -- gone outright, along with the `mfaOff` field and the startup log lines that went with them. Verified live: `ENABLE_MFA=false` overriding an environment already set to require it, every enrolment route's 403 while it is off, `ADMIN_MFA_LOCKOUT_BYPASS` letting an admin straight past a `mfaRequired: true` policy while a member is still asked, `POST /api/me/mfa/reset` refusing a non-admin and a wrong password before clearing a real enrolled factor on the right one, the settings migration (a hand-built `app.json` with the old `mfa: 'everyone'` loading as `mfaRequired: true` with the old key gone from the file after the next save), and both `HOST_MFA_REQUIRED=true` and the legacy `HOST_MFA=required` requiring enrolment at the console.
- The product's old name is gone from the pages, the tools and the documents (the author: not anywhere in the code). The product's name is configuration, `PRODUCT_NAME`, defaulting to "Coffee Pub Magpie" -- what the logo says, and one line to change. Pages carry no product name of their own: a title is the page's own name ("Sign in", "Manage") until the server's branding arrives and the environment's name is put in front of it; the umbrella "Coffee Pub" stands in the markup until then. Prose that used the old name for the platform says "the host" or "the server"; the documents say the product's name. Two window globals lost the old name (`hostText`, `appApplyCallPrefs`), and the package is `coffee-pub-magpie`. Kept, on purpose: the repository's URL and the image's name (they follow the GitHub rename), and the one line that reads a browser's old `tavern.*` keys to move them. The compose template's container names, data path and admin variables are new-install defaults; an existing install keeps its old ones, all still honoured (see the getting-started guide). The server's side (the default server name, the cookie, the data file, the variables, with compatibility) is its own entry.

### Added
- The product's brand, from the author: a **logo** (the brandmark and the words, `logo-light.png` for a dark bar and `logo-dark.png` for a light one) and a **brandmark** (the cup on its own, in colour and in white), under `public/assets/images/brand/`. The product page and the host console wear the logo in their bar; the bundled default app icon (`public/icon.png`, the favicon and the installed app's icon wherever an environment has not set its own) is the brandmark. Inside an environment the customer's own name and icon stay, as before.
- A product page at the bare base domain (`public/landing.html`), where "This is a host" stood: what it is (a call that stays open, tools beside it, tools that know each other), the eight modules, how an environment works, three plan shapes without prices yet, and a way to ask for an environment. The product's name, the contact address, the base domain and the version come from the host (`GET /api/product`, public; `PRODUCT_NAME` and `CONTACT_EMAIL` on the container), so the page never hard-codes a name while the name is still being chosen: with no contact set it says to ask whoever runs the host. Its own stylesheet (`landing.css`) on the theme's tokens; the primary nav's colour on its bar; one column on a phone. Its **Sign in** is a list of the host's environments to choose from (a public list, fine on a host run for a handful of groups), then the login and password right there: the form posts to that environment's own `/login`, a plain top-level form post, so the session is first-party and the person lands inside signed in; a refused password sends them to that environment's sign-in page with the login filled in and the usual message. An environment leaves an `env_hint` cookie on the parent domain when someone signs in there (slugs, most recent first, never a name), so the list preselects where this browser was last and the nav's Sign in goes straight there. A first version had a free-text box for the environment's name, which read as a username field and was typed into as one; replaced the same day.
- The host console (`admin.<base domain>`, `public/host.html`): the deployment's own door, with its own sign-in for a host admin (a separate kind of account, a member of no environment). It lists the environments with their plan's caps against their use (members, spaces, storage, AI calls this month, concurrent calls, the modules allowed), creates one (a slug, a name, the first owner), edits a plan, suspends and restores, backs one up as a zip, deletes one (its data is kept aside, never removed), and manages the host admins (never the last). It shows nothing of an environment beyond the counts. An environment's **Edit** (it said "Plan" at first, and the name was in there where nobody would look) changes its name and its plan; the slug is its address and stays. Built against the `/api/host/` contract in `documentation/plans/plan-tenants.md` ("Phase 1 in detail"), which the server half implements; checked against a stand-in for that API in the browser, and again once the server answered.

### Changed
- **The host is not the brand.** The object a module talks to was called `tavern` since the first module, which bound every module's code to the product's name. It is `host` now, the word the architecture always used: `host.refs`, `host.storage`, `host.toolbar.set`, `/sdk/host.js` and `/sdk/host.css`, `createHost(env)`, `document.currentScript.host || window.host`, `data-host-sdk` on the page's script tag, `[host]` in the trace, `window.hostModules` for tests. What the SDK draws inside a module is `sdk-` (`sdk-menu`...), telling it from the host's own `host-menu`. Keys and events the pages keep are `app.*` and `app:*` (`app.panels.<space>`, `app:unread`...), and a page moves anyone's old `tavern.*` keys over once, so no layout, chat history or remembered choice is lost. A clean break, no alias: there are no third-party modules yet and the product is at 0.3, so an alias would have been compatibility for nobody, carried for years; every bundled module was changed in the same sweep. The product's name stays where a name belongs (the server-name default, titles, the About tab, the repository, the data file, the session cookie), so a rebrand now touches only those. Decided in `plans/plan-modules.md` ("The host is not the brand"); the SDK and contract documents follow. Verified by every check and live: the room page, a module's own page and the drop check, all on `host`.
- Rooms are **spaces** in everything a person reads: the navigation, the pages, the buttons, the tooltips and the modules' own views ("This space" beside "Mine"). The code, the routes (`/rooms/:id`), the API fields and the stored data keep `room`, so nothing anyone has stored or linked changes; the documentation's prose follows in its own pass (see TODO.md). Done as one sweep over the pages' text and attributes and the prose in their strings, with the code left alone, then checked for anything code-like caught in it.
- The header's rows have their own colours, as theme tokens: the primary nav's middle zone is the header colour, its left and right zones a shade darker (`--nav-primary-edge-bg`), and the secondary nav (the space's bar) its own (`--nav-secondary-bg`), between the header and the page. All three derive from `--header-bg` until a theme sets them (`design-theme.md`); the theme editor does not offer them yet.
- The crumb is gone at the table: the secondary nav names the space, so the primary nav's "> Lobby" said it twice. The aside's Rejoin call button, which lived in the crumb, is in the secondary nav's right zone with the other space actions. On every other page the crumb still says where you are (Server Settings, a profile, a module's page), since those have no second row.
- The header is two rows in three zones each, and every control now has a zone by what it is about (the system or the space) and what it does (navigation, action, information), not by where it fit. The primary nav (every page): left, the logo and where you are; middle, centred on the row whatever the other zones hold, the core navigation (Rooms, each module's own page, shown with their names now rather than as bare icons); right, the system's actions and information (your profile, Manage, Install, the time on the server's clock, Sign out). The secondary nav (the room bar, at the table): left, the room's name and the module selector; middle, the space's own information and navigation (empty so far); right, the space's actions (the stage-level snap, Full screen, Pop out, Pull participants back -- moved here from the primary row, since it is the room's action -- and Leave). On a phone the primary row keeps the logo, the crumb and a menu, with the middle zone's links moved into the menu, and the secondary row is the tab bar as before. Documented in `architecture-navigation.md`; what each zone grows to hold, and modules registering tools into the bars (after the author's Blacksmith menubar), are in `documentation/plans/plan-nav.md`. Verified live: both rows measure with the middle zone at the row's centre and the right zone flush right, at the table and on the room list; the phone menu takes the core links.

### Added
- The stage-level snap: a switch in the room bar (beside Full screen) that puts every pane that can float onto the grid -- docked ones are floated first, their docked mode remembered, and any pane opened later comes up floating and snapped; off, the ones that were docked dock again -- and, while it is on, a slider for the grid's size (the grid shows while it moves, and every snapped pane refits). The grid is about half the size it was (a 130px pitch, adjustable from 60 to 320), and a pane never takes fewer cells than its smallest size needs, so a fine grid still gives a usable pane. Both are remembered with the room's layout. Verified live with the chat pane: the stage switch snapping it and setting its own switch, the slider previewing the grid and refitting the pane, releasing hiding the grid, the stage switch off, and the pane's own switch still working alone.
- Language, time and money: a new section on Manage > Settings' Server tab. **Clock** (12-hour, the default, or 24-hour) is how every time is shown across the server and every module; **Currency** is the one amounts are shown in unless a trip names its own; **Language** is the interface language (English only, until translations arrive). The SDK carries them to modules (`tavern.locale()`, `tavern.util.time`, `tavern.util.hour12`, `tavern.util.money`), and Planner (0.7.24; every time on a card, a row, a day's summary, the trip markers and the Trips widget, and money through the server's currency when the trip has none), Calendar (1.17.4, and its widget), Polls (1.12.7, and its widget) and To-do (1.11.5) follow them; before, Planner always showed 24-hour times and the others took the browser's choice. Showing a trip's own currency with a conversion beside it is left until a source of exchange rates is chosen (see TODO.md).
- Planner (0.7.22): a marker with no time marks the whole day -- "Travel day", "Free day" (two new default marker types, beside the timed ones) -- drawn as a tag in the day's header in its marker's colour rather than as a row among the day's items; clicking the tag opens its menu (edit, move, change type, remove). The day's "..." gained a "Mark the day" group that adds one at once, ahead of the timed blocks, which now read "Add free time at a time". A card folds what its face has no place for under a "More" line: the note, who is on it, the booking reference, the terminal, the platform, the room, the cost and who paid, only what the item has and the face did not show; a card someone opened stays open through redraws. The editor's placement field is **When** (it was Where for a day), and a length is any whole number of minutes (it was steps of five; minutes matter in travel).
- Research (0.2.6): cards pack like masonry -- each takes the rows its own height needs, so a short card beside a tall one leaves no hole under it, and the columns follow the pane's width -- and a **Cards / List** switch beside Mine / This room, the list being one line per item; remembered per person. `tavern.ui.viewSwitch` learned to share the toolbar between several switches (in the order they were made, a separator between), so a module's second switch does not draw over its first. Not run live here (Research needs the server's AI service on): checked as code, the switch composition in a node check of the SDK.
- A floating pane can snap to a grid. Floating was only ever free (anywhere, any size); a floating pane's titlebar now has a **Snap to a grid** toggle that makes it sit in the cells of a grid laid over the stage (cells of about 260 x 200, as many as the stage fits, a 16px gutter): dragging moves it a cell at a time, resizing grows it a cell at a time, the grid shows while it moves, and what is remembered is its cells, so a window resize re-tiles it rather than just keeping it on screen. Off, it floats freely as before; turning it on settles the pane into the nearest cells at once. Docked and window are untouched -- snap is a way of floating, not a replacement for docking -- and a pane keeps its DOM through every snap, as through a free drag. The chat and every module pane have it; documented in `architecture-room-layout.md` ("Free or snapped"). Verified live on the chat pane with the call unavailable: toggling on, a snapped drag and resize (with the grid shown), free again, snapped again, and a viewport change re-tiling it.
- Planner (0.7.21): the plan line. Anything can now sit *between* days as well as on one -- an idea near the first day, a few more near the tenth, a poll between two days, a research card near day 3 -- and the organizing rule is date, then time, then where a person placed it. Every item is in one of two places, and the model enforces it: on a day (`date`, `time`, `order`) or on the line at a joint (`after`, the day the joint follows, `''` for the head before the first day; then `order`). Nothing stored changed shape: the old Ideas column is simply the head of the line, and a between-days marker was already placed that way. So Ideas is gone as a column and the items with no day yet are drawn on the line before Day 1, the same way a marker between days always was; a card on the line is the card it would be in a day, with no time. Every joint takes anything: its + offers the same kinds the day's "..." does (plus the between-days marker types, added at once), the editor's Day field became **Where** (the days and the joints in line order, "Before the first day", "Between Oct 1 and Oct 2", "After the last day"; a marker sees only the joints), **Move to** in an item's menu lists the same, and **Back to ideas** became **Back to the line** (off its day, to the joint before it). Dropping: while anything is dragged over the plan -- one of its own items by its body, or another module's item or card -- every joint opens into a drop zone, as it did only for a marker before; a plan's own item dropped on a joint moves there, among the items already at it; another module's item dropped on one is offered "Put it here, between Oct 1 and Oct 2" through the shared drop menu. Earlier and Later on the line swap with a neighbour at the joint and hop to the next joint at the ends. A hidden day's joint still draws its items after the hidden-days badge, so nothing disappears with the day. The design and the author's mental model (what do I know, what are my options, what are my decisions) are in `documentation/plans/plan-travel.md`; `tools/check-travel.mjs` covers the placement rule. Verified live: Move to a joint and back, Back to the line, the joint's + adding a note at that joint through the editor, a To-do task dropped on a joint, a card dropped on the head of the line, a plan item dragged by its body onto a day and back onto the line before a marker.
- The SDK delivers a module's own dragged item to its own `tavern.refs.dropTarget` when it is dropped on the module itself (`over`, `leave`, `drop`, with its own pointer as `ref`), so moving an item within a module is the same code path as taking one from another; the host only ever handed a drag to the *other* modules, which had left Planner's own between-days marker drag (built on `dropTarget`) unreachable by the pointer. Documented in `api-module-sdk.md` ("Dragging"), with the one thing it implies: `leave` comes just before `drop`, so a drop zone that opens while a drag is over you must not close on `leave` until a tick later, or the drop is hit-tested against the wrong layout (found live, when a drop on a day landed on the joint below it).
- Planner (0.7.19): a "..." on every day (and on Ideas) listing everything that can be added there -- each kind the editor's tiles offer (a flight, a train, a ferry, a bus, a rental car; a stay; a restaurant, a café, a bar, a sight, a museum, a tour, a show; a note) and the time blocks -- each opening the editor on that day with the kind chosen. Typing to add was the only way in before; it stays the fast path, this is the plain one.

### Changed
- Themes come in a light and a dark version, picked with a switch beside the chooser on Manage > Theme; the built-in themes are renamed: Staying Blonde is Calming Teal, willhavebeen is Burnt Orange, and Default is Strong Coffee (the Bug fixes session, 161e53f).
- Every "..." in Tavern is the one icon, Font Awesome's `ellipsis-vertical`: the host's overflow buttons at the end of a titlebar, toolbar or action bar, the call's More, a card's own menu in Places, Research and Planner, and a poll's, which drew a text glyph. They were horizontal everywhere but the poll. `tools/check-module-window.mjs`, in `npm run check`, keeps it that way, the first mechanical rule for the module window (see `architecture-module-window.md`, "Rules"). A long menu (`tavern.menu.show`, and so the day's) scrolls instead of running off a short pane.
- To-do (1.11.4): dropping something on a task links it there, and on the list's empty space starts a task from it -- one own offer each, so the drop just does it; a menu appears only when the dropped item's own module adds something (its due date, on a day). Before, landing on a task offered both, making the common case a second click.

### Added
- Two-step sign-in (the pages; the server half is its own entries; the plan is `documentation/plans/plan-mfa.md`): a six-digit code from an authenticator app after the password. On the profile, **Two-step sign-in** turns it on (scan the QR or type the key, confirm with a code, keep the ten recovery codes shown once) and off (a code); an admin editing someone can **Reset their second factor**. Signing in gets a code page (`/login/verify`, with a recovery code as the alternative and "Remember this browser for 30 days"), or the set-up page first (`/login/enrol`) for an account whose role requires one; a personal link gets the same step. Manage > Server has the rule (off, optional, required for admins, required for everyone) and the Users tab marks accounts with a factor. The host console asks its own second step at sign-in, has the host admin's own set-up on the Host tab, and resets an owner's factor from the environment's card by login. Getting back in: `MFA_RESET` and `MFA_OFF` on the server (the compose sample and the getting-started guide say how), with a banner on Manage and the console while `MFA_OFF` is set. Asked as "how can we support mfa?", "plan it", "build it; we need a way for an admin to regain access, e.g. a flag in the server yml".
- Environments, phases 2 to 5 (the pages; the server half is its own entries): on a hosted server the environment's admin is its **owner** in every word (the Roles grid, a profile, the New user form), what only the host does (uploading a module zip, running a module in the page) is not offered to an owner, and Manage > Server starts with an **Environment** panel: the plan's name, each cap with what is used as a bar, the past-due banner with the date the environment goes to the free plan, **Upgrade** (the product page's plans), **Download a copy** (the environment as a zip) and **Ask for deletion** (a request the host carries out, withdrawable). The Modules tab's Available list marks a module the plan leaves out "Not in your plan". The host console gains a **Plans** panel (the catalog: `free` and any named plan, each with its caps), a plan picker on an environment's card that fills the caps from the catalog, past-due and deletion-requested facts on a card, and the billing webhook's address and rules under "The host". The product page lists the plans from the catalog (the free one to start, a bought one on the provider's own page) and its Get started is a sign-up form: an address checked as it is typed, a name, the owner's account, straight into the new environment. A new guide, [Your environment](documentation/userguides/userguide-environments.md), is the owner's view; the getting-started guide covers plans, sign-up and the webhook for the operator. The contract for both halves is plan-tenants.md, "Phases 2 to 5 in detail". Asked overnight as "tenants phases 2 to 5: owner role, enforcing caps, call limits, self-serve and billing".
- The host console is five tabs, the same bar Manage has: **Host** (the facts, the host admins), **Plans**, **Environments** (the default), **AI** and **Maps**, so a host with many environments reads one thing at a time. And it follows the design system properly now: environments as spaced cards with an edge in their status's colour and their facts as tiles with a bar where the plan has a cap, the Edit form as a hint, two fields grids and a row of buttons (it used to put its hint and buttons into the grid), the plan catalog as cards on the fields grid, and number fields styled like every other field (a gap in the shared stylesheet that left them white). Asked as "we need to allow the server to scale, let's add tabs; make it look nice too; there are no spaces between sections; this is laid out poorly; they should be using the unified design system".
- The space bar has a **Dock all** button beside the stage-level snap: every floating pane that can be a column goes back beside the call in one click, and the stage-level snap goes off with it (it would float a pane again the moment it opened). A pane in its own window, or one that can only float, is left alone. Asked as "we are missing a way to snap all of them back into panel view at the space level".

### Changed
- The AI keys are the host's. The host offers a **managed** AI service per company: give the server `AI_OPENAI_KEY` or `AI_ANTHROPIC_KEY` (or both), or set a key on the host console's Managed AI panel (one row per company: the key write-only, the model listed from the company with a default chosen for it, an address for an OpenAI-compatible service of your own), and that company is offered to every environment. Each environment's AI configuration page starts with a **Source** choice: one Managed entry per offered company ("Managed: OpenAI, gpt-...") or Custom (its own service and key, the fields as before). Changing the source or the company switches AI off until it is enabled again; the allowance and this month's use stay the environment's. An install that only ever used `AI_KEY` becomes managed by itself, its provider and model filling that company's slot. Asked as "the setting for the API key for AI should be at the [host] level; then they choose managed or custom", then "we have never set anything but the key; let the host offer both, `AI_ANTHROPIC_KEY` and `AI_OPENAI_KEY`, and if set you do the rest". The environment's form is `public/ai-form.js`.
- The product page's hero carries the logo, large, above the headline; the bar's small one stays as the way home. Asked as "a bigger logo in the middle".
- The map is the host's ("same with maps", "only the admin can slice"): Maps (0.7.10) declares its map-tiles folder shared with the host, so with environments there is one folder of map files every environment shows, all together, and only a host admin adds files or cuts regions, on the host console's new **Maps** panel (files with size and zoom, delete, the world map address, and Add a region, the same region cut the module's page had, now `public/region-cut.js` shared by both). An environment's Maps configuration lists those files under "Provided by the host" and chooses nothing; the map-at-a-web-address option is gone for this phase (the host's map or nothing). On a single server nothing moves: the folder, the ticks and the region cut stay on the module's page. A `files` setting may declare `"shared": "host"` in any module's manifest.
- Research (0.2.9): the tags are chosen from a **Tags** button in the toolbar (a menu of every tag in use with its count, the chosen ones ticked, and Clear tags at the top) instead of every tag in use laid out as a wall of chips under the kinds; only the chosen tags show there, each with an x to drop it. And no more pills: the kind and tag chips, a card's tags and the backlinks are small rounded rectangles (5px corners, 22px tall), the tag's colour a small square. Reported as "the tags need a tag chooser and then only show what is filtered; no more hotdog designs, rounded-corner rectangles, small". The chooser is `host.ui.toolbarButton`, new in the SDK: one button that shares the toolbar row with the view switches.

### Changed
- Hang up leaves the call but keeps the conference pane: it says "Not in a call", the toolbar's red phone turns green to dial back in, and the other toolbar buttons wait. The pane's own close (the x on its titlebar) is what closes it now, taking the call with it as before. Asked as "when not in a call I think we still show the window but with Not in a call; that means this becomes a green button to dial back in".
- Closing a pane's own window (the conference, the chat) brings the pane back into the page, docked or floating as it was before it left, with whatever it holds untouched: the call keeps going, a chat keeps its draft. Before, closing the window closed the pane, which for the conference meant leaving the call. And the conference's titlebar in its own window has no close button any more, since it read as "close this window" while it meant "leave the call"; Hang up on the toolbar leaves. Asked as "if the user closes the popped out window it should restore itself in the main browser; I do not think we show the leave-the-call x in the popout window titlebar, they can hang up to leave".

### Fixed
- An organisation-level Anthropic key answered every call with a 400, "This API key is not scoped to a workspace, so this request must include the anthropic-workspace-id header" -- found from the diagnostics just below this entry. A new optional `workspace` field (letters, digits, underscore and dash, up to 100 characters; an id, not a secret, so it is shown plainly) on the host's managed Anthropic service (`PUT`/`GET /api/host/ai`, seeded from a new `AI_ANTHROPIC_WORKSPACE` env var the same way the key is) and on an environment's own custom slot (`PUT`/`GET /api/ai`, carried through `effective()` for the managed offer too): `complete()` and `listModelsFor()` send it as the `anthropic-workspace-id` header whenever the provider is Anthropic and a workspace is set; every other provider ignores it. `tools/check-ai.mjs` gained coverage for the header being sent only for Anthropic, the field's validation, and the host's own managed-slot handling.
- Switching the AI source, company or provider always turned AI off, even between two already-working services -- reported live: with the Assistant and Research on, changing Source from Managed Anthropic to Managed OpenAI and saving answered "Assistant and Research needs the AI service; turn them off too?" and saved nothing, since `set()`/`previewEnabled()` treated any such change as turning AI off before the dependents guard ever got to ask. Now a change of source, company or provider keeps `enabled` as it was whenever the result is ready to answer with (a managed company the host offers, or a custom provider with its key); it turns `enabled` off only when the result is not ready (provider none, an unoffered managed company, a keyless custom provider) -- "setting a service up does not turn AI on" still holds, since an environment that has never enabled stays off either way. `effective()`, `managedOffer()` and the new `hasActiveService()` now take an optional candidate config, so `set()` and `previewEnabled()` can ask "would this be ready" about the patch about to be saved rather than only about what is saved already; `previewEnabled()` is rebuilt on top of the same `applyAiFields()` `set()` itself uses, rather than a hand-duplicated approximation of the rule (the old version, notably, never checked for a key at all). `tools/check-ai.mjs`'s existing coverage of switching services is updated to the new, correct behaviour rather than the old bug.
- A managed AI answer that failed said only "the AI service could not answer", with nothing to tell a 400 (a bad field) from a 404 (a model the account does not have) from a 529 (overloaded) -- and nothing logged either, so it could not be diagnosed after the fact. `server/ai.js`'s `complete()` now reads the provider's own error out of the response (Anthropic's `{ error: { type, message } }`, OpenAI's and a compatible one's `{ error: { message } }`), logs one line server-side with the provider, the model, the status and that message (never the key or the prompt), and folds the status and a sentence of the message into the answer itself: "the AI service could not answer (404: model: claude-haiku-4-5-20251001)". A refused key and a busy service keep their own plain wording, unchanged, rather than growing the provider's raw text too. The network-failure path (a timeout, a DNS failure, a refused connection) is logged and answered the same way, from the fetch error's own name and message. `tools/check-ai.mjs` gained a group driving all of this against its own stand-in service, including that the key and the prompt never reach a log line. The Anthropic request shape itself (`model`, `max_tokens`, `system`, `messages`, the `x-api-key` and `anthropic-version` headers) was checked by re-reading against the Messages API as documented, not against a live call -- no Anthropic key was available in this sandbox to place one.
- The map's search said "not configured for this space" with nothing anywhere saying where to configure it (the author: "there is no way to configure it"). Search is the place-search module's (Places) setting, chosen on that module's configuration page, and now both places say so: the map's message names the module that offers the search ("Search is not set up. It is the Places module's: choose a place search in its configuration (Manage, Modules)"), and Maps' own configuration page carries a note, **Search on the map**, saying the same. The note is a new setting type, `note`, in the module contract (`api-modules.md`): a label and a help with no control and no value, for a module to say where something is set up; the server half validates it. Maps 0.7.12.
- "Add a region" names the cut after what was typed ("thailand" becomes `thailand.pmtiles`), not after the name the place search answers with, which is often in the place's own script (Thailand came back as "ประเทศไทย", which slugged to nothing, so every cut was offered as `region.pmtiles` and the second one collided). And a new **Replace a file already there with that name** tick in the confirm block (both the console's Maps panel and a module's settings page) sends `replace: true` with the cut, for a fresh cut of the same region over the old file; the server half honours it.
- The world map address's help (the host console's Maps panel, the Maps module's "World file" setting and the Maps guide) now says what the address looks like: `https://build.protomaps.com/YYYYMMDD.pmtiles` with the build's date, an example given, and the console field's placeholder shows one. It used to say only that a daily build exists. The console's address form itself was sloppy (the author's word): the hint sat inside the label and came out in uppercase capitals over two lines, the Save button hung off the row, and the files table squeezed each file's name into the narrow first column meant for a thumbnail. It is now a label, the field with its button beside it, and the hint under them in sentence case; the file name column is as wide as it needs to be and the delete button sits at the right. Maps 0.7.11.
- "Add a region" with no world map address set said an admin needs to fix it "in Module Configuration", a place that does not exist. The shared cutter (`public/region-cut.js`) now turns the server's missing-address answer into "the world map address is not set: fill it in above and save it first", which is where the field sits on both the host console's Maps tab and a module's settings page (`wireRegionCut` takes a `sourceHint` for any page that puts it elsewhere).
- With a managed AI service chosen, Manage's AI service card said Not set up and kept Approve and enable disabled, so AI (and the Assistant, which needs it) could not be turned on; a space's AI section stayed hidden for the same reason. Both read the environment's own custom service, which is empty while the host's is in use. `GET /api/ai` now sends `ai.active`, the service in use whichever the source, and both pages read that.
- The conference popped out into a window of its own kept its titlebar and toolbar on screen for good: the idle-hide (the chrome slides away after 2.5 seconds without movement, back on the next) only knew the whole-table popout (`body.popout`) and only marked the page's own stage, not the window's. It now marks the stage the conference is wrapped in inside its window too (`roomModules.nativeWindow`), and the same slide-away rules apply to `body.conference-window`. Two more faults kept it from working on the first try ("the hiding is not working"): the timer looked up the settings popover, the reaction tray and the floating toolbar in the page, but they live inside the conference section and had moved into the window with it, so the lookup threw before anything was marked idle; and "keep the chrome while the chat is open" was meant for the whole-table popout, where the chat is in that window, not for the conference alone in a window with the chat left on the page. The timer looks in the conference's own document now, and the chat rule applies only while the call is on the page. And movement wakes only the window it happens in, so the pointer on the page no longer brings the popped-out window's chrome back ("a little odd that mousing over either shows it"). Reported as "when popped out, the video call is supposed to hide the titlebars and action bars when the mouse is not over".
- With the stage-level snap on, opening a pane that had never been opened in that space before (Calendar, say) threw on the missing saved entry (`saved[id].cell`) before the pane was registered or its module mounted: an empty pane with a header that could not be moved or closed, the switch still off, and another pane on every click; reported as "I turn on and it doesn't switch, keeps spawning windows, can't move it, close it, and it doesn't render". The cell is optional now (the snap settles from the pane's own box), in the three places that read it.
- Stream (0.1.1): the Participant box shows the Online or Offline picture (the space's own, the person's, then the server's Default Images) and falls back to the person's profile photo only when none of those is set, the rule the table's tiles already followed; before, a player with no Participant picture showed nothing. The real photo only, never the initials plate (`host.images.get(key, 'profile', { fallback: 'none' })`), and never on the Character box, which shows Character pictures alone. Asked as "make sure we are sending participant and character images, not profile images; profile only if the others are not set".
- Maps installed on a fresh environment showed a disabled Approve button; reported as "the approve button for maps was not enabled for the owner". It was not the role: Maps needs Places, and only the card said so, in small text under a button that could not be pressed. The "Available with this server" list now says "Needs Places installed and turned on first" in Maps' own row, before Install (`/api/modules`' bundled entries carry `requires`), and the Maps guide says to install Places first. Maps (0.7.9) and Places (0.8.12) settings help text still named the old product ("Tavern reads a PMTiles map file..."); it says "the server" now.
- Manage > Modules, "Available with this server": each row was a flex line with the button loose at the end, so a long description pushed Install about and short ones left it in a different place on every row; reported as "this is formatted poorly". A row is a three-column grid now (icon, name and description, Install), the buttons in one column at the right edge, a rule between rows.
